:
List of formulas introduced in the definition of a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}_{1}[\texttau].

We now associate each sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}_{1}[\texttau]
with a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-flag
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w,
that encodes whether
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma=(\textsigma\wedge\textmu)^{({\sf c})}
or

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma=(\textsigma\wedge\textmu)^{({\sf c})}.

According to this, we define the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of the Gaifman graph of the given structure to be the either the privileged component or the whole remaining graph,
depending on whether
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w=\circ
or
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w=\bullet.

The
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-flag.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}_{1}[\texttau]
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textsigma,\textmu
be its target sentences.
We define the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-flag

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
to be either the symbol “
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ
” if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma=(\textsigma\wedge\textmu)^{({\sf c})},
or the symbol “
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bullet
” if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma=\textsigma\wedge\textmu.

Every sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}_{1}[\texttau]
is associated with its
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-flag.

Choosing the privileged set given by a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-flag.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
be a graph, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q\in\mathbb{N},
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}\in(2^{V(G)})^{2q},
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
be a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(G).

Also, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w\in\{\circ,\bullet\}.

A set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
, if

C=\begin{cases}\text{an element in }{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{q},X),&\text{if }w_{i}=\circ\text{ and }{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{q},X)\neq\emptyset,\\
\emptyset,&\text{if }w_{i}=\circ\text{ and }{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{q},X)=\emptyset,\text{ and}\\
V(G)\setminus X,&\text{if }w_{i}=\bullet.\end{cases}

By
Subsection 7.3
,
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{q}
-pseudogrid of a graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
is a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(G),
then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{{q}},X)
is either a singleton or the empty set.
Therefore, we get the following:

Observation 38
.

If
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{q}
-pseudogrid of a graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
is a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(G),
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w\in\{\circ/\bullet\},
then there is exactly one
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X.

It is trivial to see that the notion of a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid and the notion of a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set can be expressed in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mbox{\sf CMSOL}.

Lemma 39
.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q\in\mathbb{N},
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
be a vocabulary, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf X},{\sf C}\}

be a set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2q+2
unary relation symbols that are not contained in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau,
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w\in\{\circ,\bullet\}.

There is a sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\sf X},{\sf C}}}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf X},{\sf C}\}]

such that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A},

every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}\in{(2^{V(\mathfrak{A})})}^{2q},
and every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X,C\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},{\bf W}_{q},X,C)\models\texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\sf X},{\sf C}}}
(where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf X}
is interpreted as
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf C}
is interpreted as
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
) if and only if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
is the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X.

The rest of this section is structured as follows:
In
Subsection 9.1
we define the
split sentence

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{q}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta,
that is a sentence equivalent to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
that separates the questions of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
that concern the non-privileged parts of the structure, and the questions that correspond to the privileged one.
Then, in
Subsection 9.2
, we define the
extended compass
of a flatness pair, that
is a tuple that contains all necessary information around a flatness pair.
Then, in
Subsection 9.3
and
Subsection 9.4
, we define the

out-signature
and the
in-signature
of
the extended compass of a flatness pair
that encodes how a partial solution (partial assignment of vertices to the variables)
satisfies the “non-privileged” and the “privileged” part of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{q},
respectively.
Finally, in
Subsection 9.5
, we present the algorithm

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\tt Fins\_Equiv\_FlatPairs}

and, in
Subsection 9.6
,
we prove that this algorithm correctly returns the claimed output of
Subsection 8.1
for the particular case of sentences in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}_{1}[\texttau].

9.1
The sentences
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}

In this subsection we aim to define two sentences
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}
that will allow us
to “break”
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
into two questions:
one that concerns the “privileged” part and one that concerns the “non-privileged” part of the graph.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q,l\in\mathbb{N},
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
be a vocabulary, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf c}
be a collection of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l
constant symbols not contained in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau,
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf R},{\sf X}\}
be a set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2q+2
unary relation symbols not contained in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}_{1}[\texttau].

Also, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
be the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-flag of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta,

let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textbeta\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}^{\bf tw}[\texttau\cup\{X\}]
be the modulator sentence of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textsigma\in{\sf FOL}[\texttau]
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textmu\in{\sf EM}[\texttau]
be the
FOL
-target and
EM
-target sentences of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta,
respectively.
Also, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textzeta=\textsigma^{l}
be the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l
-apex-projection of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textsigma
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}
be a Gaifman sentence that is equivalent to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textzeta.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}
be the enhanced version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta

obtained from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
after replacing
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textsigma
with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{{\sf ap}_{\bf c}}.

The sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}.

We define the sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf R},{\sf X}\}\cup{\bf c}]
such that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A},
every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}\in{(2^{V(\mathfrak{A})})}^{2q},

every apex-tuple
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l,
and every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X,R\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},{\bf a},X)\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}
if and only if the following conditions are satisfied:

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},X)\models\textbeta|_{{\sf star}_{\sf X}}
and

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}
and

•

if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
is the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X,
then for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime}\in{\sf cc}(G_{\mathfrak{A}},S)
that is not a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})[C^{\prime}]\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{{\sf ap}_{\bf c}}\wedge\textmu.

Note that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf R},{\sf X}\}\cup{\bf c}]
since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textbeta|_{{\sf star}_{\sf X}}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup\{{\sf X}\}]
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{{\sf ap}_{\bf c}}\in{\sf FOL}[\texttau\cup\{{\sf R}\}\cup{\bf c}].

We stress that, in the definition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}
we ask that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}
since we need to guarantee this fact in order to ask the third item, the question
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{{\sf ap}_{\bf c}}\wedge\textmu
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime}\in{\sf cc}(G_{\mathfrak{A}},S)
that is not subset of the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set (to define the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set we need
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
to be a pseudogrid).

Intuitively, if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w=\bullet,
the sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}

is the conjunction of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textbeta|_{{\sf star}_{\sf X}}
and a sentence that asks that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid, while, if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w=\circ,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}
is the conjunction of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textbeta|_{{\sf star}_{\sf X}},
a sentence that asks that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid, and a sentence that asks that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}\wedge\textmu
is satisfied in every “non-privileged” connected component.
In other words,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}

does everything that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}
does, except from the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C,
and also asks that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid.

The sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}.

We now define the sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf R}\}\cup{\bf c}]
such that

\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}=\exists{\sf X}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\framebox{$\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}$}}\ \wedge{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}\framebox{$\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\exists{\sf C}\ \texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\sf X},{\sf C}}}\ \wedge\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{{\sf ap}_{\bf c}}|_{{\sf ind}_{\sf C}}$}}\ \wedge{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\framebox{$\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\exists{\sf C}\ \texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\sf X},{\sf C}}}\ \wedge\textmu|_{{\sf ind}_{\sf C}}$}}

(6)

(recall that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf R},{\sf X}\}\cup{\bf c}],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\sf X},{\sf C}}}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf X},{\sf C}\}],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textmu\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau],
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{{\sf ap}_{\bf c}}\in{\sf FOL}[\texttau\cup\{{\sf R}\}\cup{\bf c}]
).

Alternatively, if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{{q}}\in{(2^{V(\mathfrak{A})})}^{2q},
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}
is an apex-tuple of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l,

then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},{\bf a})\models\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}\iff\exists X\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})
such that

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\framebox{$\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},X)\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}$}},

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\exists C\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})[C]\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{{\sf ap}_{\bf c}},
and

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\exists C\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}[C]\models\textmu
.

We stress that in the above sentence, the part “
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\exists{\sf C}\ \texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\sf X},{\sf C}}}
” appears twice, but essentially refers to the same set since, by
Subsection 7.3
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{A}},{\bf W}_{{q}},X)
is either a singleton or the empty set.

Intuitively, if the Gaifman graph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
contains a “big enough” wall, we can “separate” the questions that concern

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
and every non-privileged connected component of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus X
(expressed by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}
) and
the question
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{{\sf ap}_{\bf c}}\wedge\textmu
that concerns the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G

with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X.

We call
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{q}
the
split version
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

Lemma 40
.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
be a vocabulary,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf R}\notin\texttau
be a unary relation symbol, and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf c}
be a collection of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l
constant symbols, where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}_{1}[\texttau],
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{q}=({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}+1,
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}
be an enhanced version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta,
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{q}
be the split version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

If
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{{q}}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}},

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}
is an apex-tuple of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l,

then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}\iff(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},{\bf a})\models\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}.

Proof.

We will prove the lemma for the case where the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-flag
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ,
i.e.,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma=(\textsigma\wedge\textmu)^{({\sf c})},
since the
case where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w=\bullet
is trivial.
Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
be a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}),
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{{q}}
be a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}},
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}
be an apex-tuple of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l.

We first prove that if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}},
then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},{\bf a})\models\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}.

Suppose that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

By definition, this implies that there is a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},X)\models\textbeta_{{\sf star}_{\sf X}}
and for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\in{\sf cc}(G_{\mathfrak{A}},X),
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})[C]\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{{\sf ap}_{\bf c}}\wedge\textmu.

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},X)\models\textbeta_{{\sf star}_{\sf X}}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textbeta\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}^{\bf tw}[\texttau\cup\{{\sf X}\}],
we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf cl}_{\sf X}({\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{A},X))
has treewidth at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf tw}(\texttheta).

Therefore, by
Subsection 7.2
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
intersects at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}
bags of every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
-canonical partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V({\bf a}).

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid, where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q=({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}+1,

there is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\in{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{A}},{\bf W}_{q},X)
and by
Subsection 7.3

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\{C\}={\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{A}},{\bf W}_{q},X).

Thus, we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{q},{\bf a},X)\models\texttheta_{q}^{\sf out}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})[C]\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{{\sf ap}_{\bf c}}\wedge\textmu.

This implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},{\bf a})\models\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}.

To prove the inverse implication, note that if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},{\bf a})\models\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}},
then there is a
set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{q},{\bf a},X)\models\texttheta_{q}^{\sf out}
and there is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\in{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{A}},{\bf W}_{q},X)
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})[C]\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{{\sf ap}_{\bf c}}\wedge\textmu.

Thus, by the definition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{q}^{\sf out},
we deduce that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

∎

By
Subsection 9.1
, we also get the following:

Corollary 41
.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
be a vocabulary,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf R}\notin\texttau
be a unary relation symbol, and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf c}
be a collection of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l
constant symbols, where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}_{1}[\texttau],
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{q}=({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}+1,
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}
be an enhanced version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta,
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{q}
be the split version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

If
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)}
are two
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrids of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}},

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}
is an apex-tuple of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l,

then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},{\bf a})\models\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}\iff(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},{\bf a})\models\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}.

See
Table 4
for the list of formulas introduced in the last two subsections.

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\sf X},{\sf C}}}

in

9.2
Extended compasses of flatness pairs

Extended compasses of flatness pairs.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l,r\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j,q\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 3}
be two integers, such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2r+j\geq q.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
be a graph, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{l})
be an apex-tuple of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G,
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
be a flatness pair of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a})
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2r+j
.
For every subwall
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\prime}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W,
we denote by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W^{\prime})
the set of the flaps of the flat wall
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W
that either contain an edge of the perimeter of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\prime}
or are “embedded” inside the disk “cropped” by the perimeter of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\prime}.
Intuitively,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W^{\prime})
contains all flaps “captured” by the wall
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\prime}.
See
Appendix A
for a formal definition of the above notions.
The graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf compass}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W)
is always assumed to be connected.
We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K:={\sf compass}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W)
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K^{\bf a}:=G[V({\bf a})\cup V(K)].

Also, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[r],
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(i)}=V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W^{(2i+j)}))
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf I}=(I^{(1)},\ldots,I^{(r)}).

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
be a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}
be its Gaifman graph, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{l})
be an apex-tuple of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}},
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
be a flatness pair of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V({\bf a})
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2r+j.

Also, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{{q}}
be the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid defined by the horizontal and vertical paths of the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W.

We call the tuple
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{K}=(\mathfrak{A}[V(K^{\bf a})],{\bf a},{\bf I},{\bf W}_{{q}})

the extended compass
of the flatness pair
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V({\bf a})
.
Given a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z\subseteq V(K),
we define
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)
to be the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{K}(Z).

Also, if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}L\subseteq[l],
then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})
contains all non-
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\varnothing
elements in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}
indexed by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}L.

Intuitively,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{K}=(\mathfrak{A}[V(K^{\bf a})],{\bf a},{\bf I},{\bf W}_{{q}})

contains all the “useful information” around the flatness pair
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{A}).

The structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}[V(K^{\bf a})]
induced by the union of the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{R}
-compass
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W,

the apices
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf a}),
the homocentric zones of influence of
the layers of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W
(away from its
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j
-central part),
and the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}
of its “
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-central” part.

9.3
Out-signature

In this subsection we aim to “encode” all necessary information that concerns the satisfiability of the sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}
in the “non-privileged” part of the input structure.
To do this, given an extended compass of a flatness pair, we define a certain boundaried structure and we will use the finite set of representatives of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}
given by
Subsection 7.1
, to “associate” this boundaried structure with a representative.
Since parts of the “non-privileged” vertex set of the graph may lie outside the extended compass of the considered flatness pair,
we may have to “extend” the boundary of our structure in order to capture this information.
This is achieved by “guessing” all ways an (abstract) graph with a bounded number of vertices can “extend” the boundary.

Before presenting some additional definitions,
we first set up the sentences and the constants in which we will build the out-signature.
Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
be a vocabulary,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf R}\notin\texttau
be a unary relation symbol, and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf c}
be a collection of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l
constant symbols, where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}_{1}[\texttau],
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{q}:=({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}+1,
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}
be an enhanced version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta,
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{q}
be the split version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

Recall that

\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}=\exists{\sf X}\ \bigg(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\ \wedge\exists{\sf C}\Big(\texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\sf X},{\sf C}}}\ \wedge\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{\sf ap_{\bf c}}|_{{\sf ind}_{\sf C}}\Big)\wedge\exists{\sf C}\Big(\texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\sf X},{\sf C}}}\ \wedge\textmu|_{{\sf ind}_{\sf C}}\Big)\bigg)

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf R},{\sf X}\}\cup{\bf c}],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{\sf ap_{\bf c}}\in{\sf FOL}[\texttau\cup\{{\sf R}\}\cup{\bf c}],
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textmu\in{\sf EM}[\texttau].

For the rest of this subsection, keep in mind that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q=({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}+1.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime},r\in\mathbb{N}.

Also, we set

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle j

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle{\sf odd}(\max\{q/2,j^{\prime}\})\mbox{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle w

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle(r+2)\cdot q.

To give an intuition for the above, let us explain what each of the above represents:
First,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}
is the size of the wall that we want to declare irrelevant in the proof of
Subsection 8.1
.
We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q=({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}+1
in order to have a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
that defines a unique privileged component.
We will consider a wall
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2w+j
that, apart from its
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j
-central part, contains
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
“annulus buffers” of thickness
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(r+2).

We stress that while, for now,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r
is a given constant, in
Subsection 9.4
, it will be a particular constant depending on the parameters of the basic local sentences in the definition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}
).
As a modulator
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
cannot affect more than
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}=q-1
of these
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
“annulus buffers” (
Subsection 7.2
), one of them will not be affected by the solution, and therefore it will be altogether in its privileged component.

Guessing an extension of a vertex set.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
be a graph.
Given a set of vertices
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}S\subseteq V(G)
and an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell\in\mathbb{N},
we define the collection of graphs
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal F}^{S}_{\ell},
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F\in{\cal F}^{S}_{\ell}
if and only if there exists a graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}
on
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\max\{\ell-|S|,0\}
vertices and a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}E
of edges each with one endpoint in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}S
and the other endpoint in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F^{\prime}),
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F=F^{\prime}\cup(S\cup V(F^{\prime}),E)
(see
Figure 9
for an example). We stress that, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F\in{\cal F}^{S}_{\ell},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F[S]
is an edgeless graph.

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}S
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}E

Figure 9
:
A set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}S
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}6
vertices of a graph, a graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}
on
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}5
vertices and a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}E
of edges between vertices of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}S
and of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F^{\prime}).
The graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(F^{\prime}\cup(S\cup V(F^{\prime}),E)
belongs to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal F}^{S}_{11}.

Notice that if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell\leq|S|
then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal F}^{S}_{\ell}
contains only the graph with vertex set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}S
and no edges.

Towards constructing a boundaried structure.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q,j^{\prime},j,r,w,
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l
as above.
Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
be a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}
be its Gaifman graph, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{l})
be an apex-tuple of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}},
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
be a flatness pair of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V({\bf a})
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2w+j,
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{{q}}
be the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid defined by the horizontal and vertical paths of the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{q}
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W.

Also, let

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{K}=(\mathfrak{A}[V(K^{\bf a})],{\bf a},{\bf I},{\bf W}_{{q}})
be the extended compass
of the flatness pair
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V({\bf a})
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell\in[0,{\bf tw}(\texttheta)-1].

Given a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}d\in[r,w],
an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}L\subseteq[l],
a vertex set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z\subseteq I^{(d-r+1)},
and a graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F\in{\cal F}^{V_{L}({\bf a})}_{\ell},

we define the graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K^{(d,Z,L,F)}
as the one obtained from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{K^{\bf a}[I^{(d)}\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]\cup F}
by making every vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F\setminus V_{L}({\bf a}))
adjacent to an arbitrarily chosen vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d)}\setminus Z.

We also define the structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{(d,Z,L,F)}
to be the one obtained from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}[I^{(d)}\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]
after adding
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}|V(F)|
new elements to its universe and a binary relation symbol that is interpreted as pairs of elements (corresponding to the additional edges of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F
and every edge between a vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F\setminus V_{L}({\bf a}))
and a vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d)}\setminus Z
).
Notice that if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
is the Gaifman graph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A},
then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K^{(d,Z,L,F)}
is the Gaifman graph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{(d,Z,L,F)}.

For simplicity, we will assume that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{(d,Z,L,F)}
is also
a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure.

See
Figure 10
for a visualization of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K^{(d,Z,L,F)}.

Intuitively, it contains the influence of the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}d
-layer
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d)},
the apices that we guess that will belong to the modulator
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X,

the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z
that is the part of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d-r)}
that will not belong to the privileged component after the removal of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X,

and the
part
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F
that corresponds to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out},
i.e., the portion of the modulator
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
that will not be part of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d)}.

The graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F
in
Figure 10
is the graph containing all vertices
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})
and the “extra” guessed part
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}
together with the extra edges from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F^{\prime})
to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a}).

Let us explain the motivation behind adding these extra edges:
The reason we consider the structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{(d,Z,L,F)}
is to “focus” inside
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d)}
and temporarily “forget” what happens outside
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d)}.

However, we need to keep record of the fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d)}
is in the same connected component as
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F^{\prime}).
This is why we add the extra edges.

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d)}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}

Figure 10
:
The graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K^{(d,Z,L,F)}.

Strongly isomorphic graphs.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
be a graph.
A
nice 3-partition
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
is an ordered partition
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal V}=(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(G)
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(V_{1}\cup V_{2},V_{2}\cup V_{3})
is a separation of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
(see
Figure 11
for an example).

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{1}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{2}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{3}

Figure 11
:
An example of a nice
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}3
-partition
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
of a graph.

For every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell\in\mathbb{N},
let

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle{\cal H}^{(\ell)}=\{(H,{\cal V})\mid H\mbox{\penalty\ is a graph on $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell$ vertices and $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal V}$ is a nice 3-partition of $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}H$}\}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}H
be two graphs and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal V}=(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal U}=(U_{1},U_{2},U_{3})

be nice 3-partitions of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}H,
respectively.
We say that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
is
strongly isomorphic to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}H
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\cal V},{\cal U})
, if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
is isomorphic to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}H,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G[V_{1}\cup V_{2}]
is isomorphic to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}H[U_{1}\cup U_{2}],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G[V_{2}\cup V_{3}]
is isomorphic to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}H[U_{2}\cup U_{3}],
and these two last isomorphisms are identical when restricted to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{2}.

Let us exlain why we introduce strongly isomorphic graphs.
Having defined the structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{(d,Z,L,F)},
we aim to define a boundaried structure that we will associate with a representative of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.

The boundary of our boundaried structure will be the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)\cup V(F).
By definition, the graph induced by this set has an obvious nice
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}3
-partition (since there is no edge between
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)
).
The information we want to store is not just a boundary but the “inner-structure” of this boundary, which is mirrored by the nice 3-partition. We demand this “stronger” notion of isomorphism to be able to find another boundaried structure that corresponds to the same representative of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}
and still its boundary is “nicely 3-partitioned” in the same way as the boundary of the initial boundaried structure, since (as we will see later in the course of the proof) the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F)
remains “invariant” no matter of which flatness pair the extended compass we consider - thus, our isomorphism needs to keep
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F)
“intact”.

The out-signature of an extended compass.

We now define the out-signature of an extended compass.
We encode all possible sets
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F\in{\cal F}^{V_{L}({\bf a})}_{i-|\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)|},
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[0,{\bf tw}(\texttheta)-1],
and all representatives
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textphi}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}
such that when extending
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)\cup V_{L}({\bf a})
to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)\cup V(F),

the boundaried structure obtained from

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{(d,Z,L,F)}

after considering
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)\cup V(F)

as its boundary,
satisfies
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textphi}.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}:=\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf R},{\sf X}\}\cup{\bf c}
and, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell\in[0,{\bf tw}(\texttheta)-1],

following
Subsection 7.1
,
we consider the collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf rep}_{\texttau^{\prime}}^{(\ell)}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q})
of sentences on
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structures that are “representatives” of the sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}
(that is a sentence in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau^{\prime}]
).

We set

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf SIG}_{\sf out}}:=\{({\bf H},\bar{\textphi})\mid\exists\ell\in[0,{\bf tw}(\texttheta)-1]\mbox{\rm\penalty\ such that $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf H}\in{\cal H}^{(\ell)}$\penalty\ and\penalty\ }\bar{\textphi}\in{{\sf rep}^{(\ell)}_{\texttau^{\prime}}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q})}\}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{K}=(\mathfrak{A}[V(K^{\bf a})],{\bf a},{\bf I},{\bf W}_{{q}})
be the extended compass of a flatness pair
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a})
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2w+j,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\subseteq V(K^{\bf a}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}d\in[r,w],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}L\subseteq[l],
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z\subseteq I^{(d-r+1)}.

We define

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm in}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf a})\setminus V_{L}({\bf a})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
“non-privileged” connected
components of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus X
.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}

Figure 12
:
The set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
and the connected components of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus X.

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}}(\mathfrak{K},R,d,L,Z)=\{({\bf H},\bar{\textphi})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf SIG}_{\sf out}}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\mid

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\exists\ F\in{\cal F}^{V_{L}({\bf a})}_{|V(H)|-|\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)|},\mbox{ such that if $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf H}=(H,{\cal U})$}

(7)

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal V}=(\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z),V_{L}({\bf a}),V(F)\setminus V_{L}({\bf a})),
then

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal V}
is a nice 3-partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]\cup F

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]\cup F
is strongly isomorphic

to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}H
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\cal V},{\cal U}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\exists
an ordering
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)\cup V(F),
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\exists\ \tilde{X}\subseteq Z\cup V(F)\mbox{\penalty\ such that\penalty\ }\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)\cup V(F)\subseteq\tilde{X}\mbox{\penalty\ and}

if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R^{\prime}=(Z\setminus\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z))\cap R,
then

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \big(\mathfrak{A}^{(d,Z,L,F)},R^{\prime},{\bf W}_{q},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X},{\bf b}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}\}.

Intuitively,
for each
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf H}\in{\cal H}^{(\ell)},
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell\in[0,{\bf tw}(\texttheta)-1]
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf H}
is a graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}H
together with a nice
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}3
-partition,
and each
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textphi}\in{\sf rep}^{(\ell)}_{\texttau^{\prime}}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}),

we are asked to guess three
objects:
a graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F\in{\cal F}^{V_{L}({\bf a})}_{|V(H)|-|\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)|},

a function
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textrho
and
a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X.

The guessed additional part
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F

represents the boundary of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}
that is the portion of the modulator that will be away from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d)}.

The set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)\cup V(F)

is the boundary of the boundaried structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{(d,Z,L,F)},R^{\prime},{\bf W}_{q},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm in},{\bf b}\big).

This
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}|\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)\cup V(F)|
-boundaried structure should
be a model of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textphi}
and its boundary
(that is the union of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]
) should be isomorphic to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}H.

The ordering
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}
of the boundary is guessed.
In
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z,
we will guess
the portion
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}
of the solution that will be part of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d)}
(see
Figure 12
and
Figure 13
for the situation of these sets inside the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}d
-layer).

Also, keep in mind that, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}
is a sentence in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf R},{\sf X}\}\cup{\bf c}]
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textphi}\in{{\sf rep}^{(\ell)}_{\texttau^{\prime}}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q})},

we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textphi}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf R},{\sf X}\}\cup{\bf c}\cup\{{\sf b}_{1},\ldots,{\sf b}_{\ell}\}],

where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf b}_{1},\ldots,{\sf b}_{\ell}
are constant symbols different than the symbols in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf c}.

When asking whether
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{(d,Z,L,F)},R^{\prime},{\bf W}_{q},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X},{\bf b}\big)\models\bar{\textphi},
we interpret
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf c}
by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\varnothing^{l}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf b}_{1},\ldots,{\sf b}_{\ell}
by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}.

We stress that, in the out-signature of every extended compass, we always interpret
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf c}
by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\varnothing^{l}.

This choice will be supported by the assumption of
Subsection 8.1
, that the neighborhood of the apices of the input flatness pair
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
is asked to have “big enough” bidimensionality with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R}),

and therefore, as we will prove in
Subsection 9.6
,
every apex vertex should be either contained in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(X)
or inside the privileged component.

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d)}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}r}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}Z}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm in}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf a})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}j}

Figure 13
:
An example of a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d)}
inside the extended compass of a flatness pair of a given graph and the position of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z,{X}_{\rm in},X_{\rm out},V_{L}({\bf a}),
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}.

In the proof of
Subsection 8.1
, we will find two extended compasses
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{K},R),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{K}^{\prime},R^{\prime})
with the same
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}}
for a particular choice of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}d
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}L
and some choices
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z^{\prime},
respectively.
In the proof,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z^{\prime}

will be exchanged as well as the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}
’s inside those
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z^{\prime}.

Here it is important to notice that the graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F
is always the same (for both
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{K},R)
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{K}^{\prime},R^{\prime})
)
and constitutes the fictitious “invariant” part of the graph, that is not affected during this exchange.
See
Figure 12
for the great picture –– what is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F
will not be exchanged, while
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z)
will be substituted by the isomorphic
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}^{\prime}}(Z^{\prime})
(see also
Figure 19
).

9.4
In-signature

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{q}
be the split version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}},
as in the previous subsection.
Recall that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}\in{\sf FOL}[\texttau^{\langle{\bf c}\rangle}\cup\{{\sf R}\}].

there exist
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}p\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{p},r_{1},\ldots,r_{p}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1},
and sentences
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\textzeta}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\textzeta}_{p}\in{\sf FOL}[\texttau^{\langle{\bf c}\rangle}\cup\{{\sf R}\}]
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}
is a Boolean combination of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\textzeta}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\textzeta}_{p}
and for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h\in[p],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\textzeta}_{h}
is a basic local sentence with parameters
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell_{h}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{h},
i.e.,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\tilde{\textzeta}_{h}=\exists{\sf x}_{1}\ldots\exists{\sf x}_{\ell_{h}}\big(\bigwedge_{i\in[\ell_{h}]}{\sf x}_{i}\in{\sf R}\wedge\bigwedge_{1\leq i<j\leq\ell_{h}}d({\sf x}_{i},{\sf x}_{j})>2r_{h}\wedge\bigwedge_{i\in[\ell_{h}]}\textpsi_{h}({\sf x}_{i})\big),

where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textpsi_{h}
is an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{h}
-local formula in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf FOL}[\texttau^{\langle{\bf c}\rangle}]
with one free variable.
Keep in mind that, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}\in{\sf FOL}[\texttau^{\langle{\bf c}\rangle}\cup\{{\sf R}\}],
distances are measured in the Gaifman graph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\langle{\bf c}\rangle}
-structures.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{r}:=\max_{h\in[p]}\{r_{h}\},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{\ell}:=\max_{h\in[p]}\{\ell_{h}\},
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r:=2\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)\cdot\hat{r}.

As in the previous subsection,
we keep in mind that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q=({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}+1,
we let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N},
and we set

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle j

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle{\sf odd}(\max\{q/2,j^{\prime}\})\mbox{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle w

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle(r+2)\cdot q.

The reason that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r
is set to be equal to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)\cdot\hat{r}
will be clear in the proof of
Subsection 8.1

and is based on an idea already present in
[
53
]
.

Scattered sets in structures.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
be a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}).

We say that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell,r)
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
, if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}|X|=\ell
and for every two distinct vertices in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X,
their distance in the Gaifman graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}
is more than
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2r,
i.e., for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a,b\in X,a\neq b,
it holds that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}d^{\mathfrak{A}}(a,b)>2r.

The in-signature of an extended compass.

We now define the in-signature of an extended compass.
In this, using the approach of
[
53
]
,
we encode all (partial) sets of variables, one set for each basic local sentence of the Gaifman sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R},
such that these variables are lying inside an “inner part” of the compass, they are scattered in this inner part, and they satisfy the local formulas
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textpsi_{i}.

These arguments are always applied in some
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\langle\bf c\rangle}
-structure of the form
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{\bf c}(\mathfrak{A},{\bf a}).

We define

{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf SIG}_{\sf in}}=2^{[\ell_{1}]}\times\cdots\times 2^{[\ell_{p}]}\times[w].

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{K}=(\mathfrak{A}[V(K^{\bf a})],K^{\bf a},{\bf a},{\bf I},{\bf W}_{{q}})
be an extended compass of the flatness pair
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a})
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2w+j,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\subseteq V(K^{\bf a}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}d\in[r,w],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}L\subseteq[l],
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z\subseteq I^{(d-r+1)}.

We set

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf in\mbox{-}sig}}(\mathfrak{K},R,d,L,Z)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\{(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{p},t)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf SIG}_{\sf in}}\mid t\leq d\mbox{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }\exists\ C\in{\sf pr}(K^{\bf a}[I^{(d)}],{\bf W}_{q},Z)\mbox{\penalty\ and}

(8)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \exists\ (\tilde{X}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{X}_{p})\mbox{\penalty\ such that\penalty\ }\forall h\in[p]\

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \tilde{X}_{h}=\{x_{i}^{h}\mid i\in Y_{h}\},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \tilde{X}_{h}\subseteq(I^{(t-\hat{r}+1)}\setminus Z)\cap R,\text{ and}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \text{if\penalty\ }{\bf a}^{\prime}={\bf a}\setminus V_{L}({\bf a}),\text{ then}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \tilde{X}_{h}\text{\penalty\ is\penalty\ }(|Y_{h}|,r_{h})\text{-scattered in }{\sf ap}_{\bf c}(\mathfrak{A},{\bf a}^{\prime})[I^{(t)}\setminus Z]

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \text{and }{\sf ap}_{\bf c}(\mathfrak{A},{\bf a^{\prime}})[C]\models\bigwedge_{x\in\tilde{X}_{h}}\textpsi_{h}(x)\}.

Here,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
represents the “privileged” part of the graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K^{\bf a}[I^{(d)}]
obtained after the removal of the whole
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z
(that is the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm in}
together with all the non-privileged components of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus X
that are connected with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm in}
).
Notice that this is a more restricted part of the privileged component of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus X
but it is also “flat”.
Then, we guess how the scattered sets of each of the basic local sentences of the Gaifman sentence can intersect this graph (a buffer that “crops” the area that contains the vertices that intersect an inner-area of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
corresponds to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t,
and the numbers of the selected vertices correspond to the sets
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{p}
) and how these variables satisfy the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{i}
-local formulas
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textpsi_{i}.

The “scatteredness” and the satisfaction of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textpsi_{i}
are evaluated on the structure after “projecting” with respect to the apices.

We finally define

{\sf CHAR}={[r,w]}\times 2^{[l]}\times 2^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf SIG}_{\sf out}}}\times 2^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf SIG}_{\sf in}}}

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K},R)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle=

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\{(d,L,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf sig}_{\sf out}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf sig}_{\sf in}})\in{\sf CHAR}\mid\exists\ Z\subseteq I^{(d-r+1)}\mbox{\penalty\ such that},

(9)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}}(\mathfrak{K},R,d,L,Z)={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf sig}_{\sf out}},\mbox{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf in\mbox{-}sig}}(\mathfrak{K},R,d,L,Z)={\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf sig}_{\sf in}}\}.

Observe that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}|{\sf CHAR}|={\cal O}_{|\texttheta|,l,q,j^{\prime}}(1).

9.5
An algorithm for finding equivalent flatness pairs

In this subsection we present an algorithm
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\tt Find\_Equiv\_FlatPairs}
that will serve as the algorithm for
Subsection 8.1
.
Given the inputs in
Subsection 8.1
,
the algorithm
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\tt Find\_Equiv\_FlatPairs}
will return, in linear time,
a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})\setminus V({\bf a})
and a flatness pair
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W^{\prime},\mathfrak{R}^{\prime})
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V({\bf a})
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}g

that is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\prime\prime}
-tilt of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
for a subwall
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\prime\prime}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W,
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
is the input
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}
is an apex-tuple of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A},
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
is a flatness pair of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V({\bf a})
of “sufficiently big” height,
with the property that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}\iff(\mathfrak{A}\setminus V({\sf compass}_{\mathfrak{R}^{\prime}}(W^{\prime})),R\setminus Y,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

The proof of correctness of the algorithm
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\tt Find\_Equiv\_FlatPairs}
will prove
Subsection 8.1
and it is in
Subsection 9.6
.

Before presenting the the algorithm
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\tt Find\_Equiv\_FlatPairs},
we present
Table 5
that summarizes all different formulas that we consider up to this point, with their corresponding meanings.

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r
-local formulas of the basic local sentences of the Gaifman sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}
