:
List of formulas used in the proof of
Subsection 8.1
for sentences in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}_{1}[\texttau]
with their respective meanings.

The algorithm
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\tt Find\_Equiv\_FlatPairs}.

The algorithm has four steps.
First, recall that
there exist
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}p\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{p},r_{1},\ldots,r_{p}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1},
and sentences
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\textzeta}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\textzeta}_{p}\in{\sf FOL}[\texttau^{\langle{\bf c}\rangle}\cup\{{\sf R}\}]
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}
is a Boolean combination of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\textzeta}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\textzeta}_{p}
and for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h\in[p],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\textzeta}_{h}
is a basic local sentence with parameters
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell_{h}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{h},
i.e.,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\tilde{\textzeta}_{h}=\exists{\sf x}_{1}\ldots\exists{\sf x}_{\ell_{h}}\big(\bigwedge_{i\in[\ell_{h}]}{\sf x}_{i}\in{\sf R}\wedge\bigwedge_{1\leq i<j\leq\ell_{h}}d({\sf x}_{i},{\sf x}_{j})>2r_{h}\wedge\bigwedge_{i\in[\ell_{h}]}\textpsi_{h}({\sf x}_{i})\big),

where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textpsi_{h}
is an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{h}
-local formula in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf FOL}[\texttau^{\langle{\bf c}\rangle}]
with one free variable.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{r}:=\max_{h\in[p]}\{r_{h}\}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{\ell}:=\max_{h\in[p]}\{\ell_{h}\}.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}c
to be the size of the
FOL
-target sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textsigma
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle q

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}+1,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\hyperref@@ii[@riguardavano]{f_{\ref*{@riguardavano}}}(|\texttheta|,{\bf tw}(\texttheta),g)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=\max\{q,(g+1)^{2}+1\},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\hyperref@@ii[@carthaginoise]{f_{\ref*{@carthaginoise}}}(|\texttheta|,{\bf tw}(\texttheta))

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=q-1,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle j^{\prime}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=g+2\hat{r}+2,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle j

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:={\sf odd}(\max\{q/2,j^{\prime}\}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle r

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=2\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)\cdot\hat{r},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle w

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=(r+2)\cdot q,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle m

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=2^{|{\sf CHAR}|}\cdot q\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3),\text{ and}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\hyperref@@ii[@occidentales]{f_{\ref*{@occidentales}}}({\bf hw}(\texttheta),{\bf tw}(\texttheta),c,l,g)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=\lceil(2w+j)\cdot\sqrt{m}\rceil.

Step 1
:
We first find a “packing” of subwalls of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W,
i.e., a collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal W}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}m

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(2w+j)
-subwalls of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W
such that their influences are pairwise disjoint.
This collection exists because
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W
has height at least
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hyperref@@ii[@occidentales]{f_{\ref*{@occidentales}}}({\bf hw}(\texttheta),{\bf tw}(\texttheta),|\textsigma|,l,j^{\prime})=\lceil(2w+j)\cdot\sqrt{m}\rceil

and because, due to
Subsection A.4
, for every distinct
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{i},W_{j}\in{\cal W},
there are no cells of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{R}
that are both
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{i}
-perimetric and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{j}
-perimetric.
Observe that the collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal W}
can be computed in linear time.

Step 2
:
Then, for every wall
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{i}\in{\cal W},
we compute a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{i}
-tilt of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R}),
which we denote by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{i},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}),
and we consider the collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}:=\{(\tilde{W}_{i},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i})\mid W_{i}\in{\cal W}\}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}m
flatness pairs of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V({\bf a})
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2w+j.

Note that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}
can be computed in time
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal O}(n),
due to
Subsection A.4
.

Step 3
:
For every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[m],
let

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{i}:={\sf compass}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}}(\tilde{W}_{i}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{i}^{{\bf a}}:=G_{\mathfrak{A}}[V({\bf a})\cup V(K_{i})],
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(i)}
be the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid defined by the horizontal and the vertical paths of the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{i}.

Also, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}d\in[w],
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{i}^{(d)}:=V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}}(W_{i}^{(2d+j^{\prime})}))
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf I}_{i}:=(I_{i}^{(1)},\ldots,I_{i}^{(w)}).

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{K}_{i}:=(\mathfrak{A}[V(K_{i}^{\bf a})],{\bf a},{\bf I}_{i},{\bf W}^{(i)}_{{q}})
be the extended compass of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{i},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i})
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V({\bf a}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{i}:=R\cap V(K_{i}^{\bf a}),
and observe that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i,j\in[m],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{i}\cap R_{j}=R\cap V({\bf a}).

After defining the above collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\{(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1}),\ldots,(\mathfrak{K}_{m},R_{m})\}
of extended compasses of flatness pairs of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V({\bf a}),
we compute their characteristics:
Since, by the hypothesis of the lemma,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{i},i\in[m]
has treewidth at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}z,
by Courcelle’s theorem (
Subsection 1.2
),
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{i},R_{i})
can be computed in time
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal O}_{|\texttheta|}(n).

We say that two flatness pairs
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{i},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}),(\tilde{W}_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{j})\in\tilde{\cal W}
are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{i},R_{i})=\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{j},R_{j}).

Step 4
:
Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}m=2^{|{\sf CHAR}|}\cdot q\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)
and for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[m],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{i},R_{i})\subseteq{\sf CHAR},

we can find a collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}\subseteq\tilde{\cal W}
of pairwise

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent flatness pairs
such that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}|\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}|=q\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3).

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1})\in\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{W}
to be the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{1},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\bullet}
to be the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}g
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{1},
and keep in mind that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}=g+2\hat{r}+2.

Note that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{W}
(resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\bullet}
) is also the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}
-subwall (resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}g
-subwall) of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{1}
and, therefore, it is a subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}
(resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}g
).
Again, using
Subsection A.4
, we compute, in time
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal O}(n),

a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{W}
-tilt

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\breve{W}^{\prime},\breve{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime})

of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})

and
a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\bullet}
-tilt

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}^{\prime},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime})

of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R}).

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y:=V({\sf compass}(\breve{W}^{\prime},\breve{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime})).

We output the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y
and the flatness pair

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}^{\prime},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime}).

Observe that the overall algorithm runs in linear time.

9.6
Proof of correctness of the algorithm for sentences in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}_{1}

In order to complete the proof of
Subsection 8.1
for a sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}_{1}[\texttau],

we have to prove that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}\iff(\mathfrak{A}\setminus V({\sf compass}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime}}(\tilde{W}^{\prime})),R\setminus Y,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

For sake of simplicity, we use
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
to denote the Gaifman graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}.

Observations on the collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}.

Recall that, for every two
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{i},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}),(\tilde{W}_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{j})\in\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{i},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i})
(resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{j})
) is a

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{i}
-tilt (resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{j}
-tilt) of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R}),

where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{i},W_{j}\in{\cal W}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W_{i}))\cap V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W_{j}))=\emptyset.

This implies that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\sf compass}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}}(\tilde{W}_{i}))\cap V({\sf compass}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{j}}(\tilde{W}_{j}))=\emptyset.

Moreover, observe that
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
is a

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
-canonical partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a}),

then no internal bag of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
intersects both
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W_{i}))
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W_{j})),
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i,j\in[m],i\neq j.

Shifting to the split version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

Suppose that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

Recall that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}
is the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid defined by the horizontal and the vertical paths of the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{1}.

Due to
Subsection 9.1
, we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},{\bf a})\models\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}},

i.e.,
there is a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})
such that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},{\bf a},X)\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}
and there is a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})
that is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X

and it holds that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}[C]\models\textmu

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})[C]\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{\sf ap_{\bf c}}.

Notice that, due to
Section 9
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
is unique.

Finding a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent extended compass that is disjoint from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X.

Recall that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}
is a collection of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)
flatness pairs of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a})
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2w+j
that are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1}).

The fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf star}(\mathfrak{A},X)\models\textbeta
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textbeta\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}^{\bf tw}[\texttau\cup\{{\sf X}\}]
implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf cl}_{\sf X}({\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{A},X))
has treewidth at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf tw}(\texttheta).

Therefore, by
Subsection 7.2
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
intersects at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}=q-1
internal bags of every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
-canonical partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a}).

This, together with the fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}|\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}|=q\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)

and that,
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
is a

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
-canonical partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a}),

then no internal bag of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
intersects both
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W_{i}))
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W_{j})),
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i,j\in[m],i\neq j,

implies that there is a collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{\ell}+2
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1})\notin\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime\prime},
every flatness pair in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime\prime}
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1}),
and the vertex set of its influence is disjoint from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X.

Assume, without loss of generality, that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{2},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{2})\in\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime\prime},
which implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1})=\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{2},R_{2})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(w)}\cap X=\emptyset.

Every modulator leaves an intact buffer.

We fix
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
to be a

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1})
-canonical partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a}).

By
Subsection 7.2
,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
intersects at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q-1
bags of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}.

This implies that, given that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{1}
has height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2w+j
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w=(r+2)\cdot q,

there is an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[q]
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X\cap(I_{1}^{(i\cdot r-1)}\setminus I_{1}^{(i\cdot r-r)})=\emptyset.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm in}=X\cap I_{1}^{(i\cdot r-r)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}=X\setminus I_{1}^{(i\cdot r-1)}
(see
Figure 14
for a visualization of an example).
We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}d:=i\cdot r-1.

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}X_{\rm in}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{1,0.549,0}X_{\rm out}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}r}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}r}

Figure 14
:
The wall
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{1}
together with some “zones” of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r
consecutive layers.
The area bounded by the orange layer corresponds to the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{1}^{(i\cdot r)},
while the area bounded by the green layer corresponds to the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{1}^{(i\cdot r-r+1)}.

The sets
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm in}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}
are depicted with blue and orange, repectively.
With light blue (resp. pink) we depict the “non-privileged” connected components of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus X
that are adjacent to vertices of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm in}
(resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}
).

Picking the privileged component inside
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus X.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}
be the privileged connected component of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z=I_{1}^{(d-r+1)}\setminus\breve{C}
and observe that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\subseteq X_{\rm in}
(in
Figure 14
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z
corresponds to the union of the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm in}
and all connected components of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus X
that are depicted in yellow).
We stress that, the target sentences are asked to be satisfied in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
but, depending on the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-flag of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
is either equal to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}
or
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\mathfrak{A})\setminus X.

Note that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{1}^{(d-r+1)}\setminus\breve{C}
is the union of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm in}
and of every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\in{\sf cc}(G,X)
that contains a vertex that is adjacent to a vertex of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm in}.

Therefore, if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w=\circ,
for each
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\in{\sf cc}(G,X)
that is a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z
(that is equal to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{1}^{(d-r+1)}\setminus\breve{C}
), we ask that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})[C]\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{\sf ap_{\bf c}},
while, if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w=\bullet,
we ask that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})\setminus X\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{\sf ap_{\bf c}}
and we keep in mind that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z\setminus X\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})\setminus X.

All apices are adjacent to the privileged component.

We also set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})=X\cap V({\bf a})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}L
be the set of indices of the vertices of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X.

Observe that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})\subseteq X_{\rm out}.

We also claim that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})=V({\bf a})\cap N_{G}(\breve{C}).

More generally, we show that for every set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}
that
intersects at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q-1
bags of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q},

if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime}
is the privileged component of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime},

then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})=V({\bf a})\cap N_{G}(C^{\prime}).

Suppose that a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})
intersects at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q-1
bags of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}.

By assumption,
every vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf a})
is adjacent, in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G,
to at least
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
internal bags of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}.

Therefore, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a\in V({\bf a}),
there is an internal
bag
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Q
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(Q)\subseteq V(G\setminus X^{\prime})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a
is adjacent, in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G,
to a vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(Q).

For every such
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Q,
since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime}
is the privileged component of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime},

it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(Q)\subseteq C^{\prime}
and therefore
every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a\in V({\bf a})
is adjacent, in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G,
to a vertex in

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime}.

This implies that every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a\in V({\bf a})
is either in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G}(C^{\prime})
(that is a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
) or belongs to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime}.

Therefore
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})=V({\bf a})\cap N_{G}(C^{\prime}).

Hence, in the particular case of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C},
we have that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})=V({\bf a})\cap N_{G}(\breve{C}).

Note that, since for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})
that
intersects at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q-1
bags of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
it holds that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a\in V({\bf a})
is either in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G}(C^{\prime})
(that is a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
) or belongs to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime},
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime}
is the privileged component of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime},
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime\prime}\in{\sf cc}(G,X^{\prime})
that is not privileged, it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf a}\cap V(C^{\prime\prime})=\emptyset.

Therefore, whenever we assume that, given a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-stracture
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A},
an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}),
a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}\in{(2^{V(\mathfrak{A})})}^{2q},
an apex-tuple
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l
, and a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{q},{\bf a},X)\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},
we can replace
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}
with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\varnothing^{l}
without affecting the validity of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.

The fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1})=\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{2},R_{2})
implies that there is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z^{\prime}\subseteq I_{2}^{(d-r+1)},
such that

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1},d,L,Z)={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}}(\mathfrak{K}_{2},R_{2},d,L,Z^{\prime})
and

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf in\mbox{-}sig}}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1},d,L,Z)={\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf in\mbox{-}sig}}(\mathfrak{K}_{2},R_{2},d,L,Z^{\prime}).

We first prove the following:

Claim 1.

There is a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}\subseteq Z^{\prime}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\subseteq X^{\prime}
and for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V\subseteq Y

that is also a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\breve{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime}}(\overline{W}),
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\overline{W}
is the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(j^{\prime}-2)
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{1},
it holds that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},X)\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\iff(\mathfrak{A}\setminus V,R\setminus Y,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.

Proof of
Claim 1
:

The idea here is to build an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structure (with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{1}
) to fit the
out
-
sig
.
By
Subsection 7.1
, this structure is associated with a sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textphi}\in{\sf rep}_{\texttau^{\prime}}^{(h)}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}).

Next, we will consider another
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structure (with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{2}
) that satisfies the same sentence, using the fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{1}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{2}
have the same
out
-
sig
.
The fact that both these boundaried structures, when “completed” from the other side by the same structure, give the same structure,
will imply that they are “equivalent” with respect to the satisfaction of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h:=|N_{G}(\breve{C})|.

Recall that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf cl}_{\sf X}({\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{A},X))
has treewidth at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf tw}(\texttheta).

Since the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G}(\breve{C})
induces a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{h}
on the Gaifman graph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf cl}_{\sf X}({\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{A},X)),
we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h\in[0,{\bf tw}(\texttheta)-1].

Defining the boundary of our boundaried structure.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}
to be the graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G[(X_{\rm out}\setminus V_{L}({\bf a}))\cap N_{G}(\breve{C})].

In other words,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}
is the subgraph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
induced by the vertices of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}
that are not apices and are adjacent to vertices in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}
(see
Figure 12
for an example).
Also, we set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\star}
to be the graph obtained from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G[V_{L}({\bf a})\cup V(F^{\prime})]
after removing every edge that has both endpoints in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a}).

Intuitively, we extend
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}
to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\star}
by adding the vertices in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})
and the edges connecting vertices of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F^{\prime}),
but not the edges that have both endpoints in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a}).

This graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\star}
will be later associated with a graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F\in{\cal F}_{h-|\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)|}^{V_{L}({\bf a})}.

Separating
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
into two boundaried structures.

We now aim to “break”
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
into two boundaried structures,
to be able to encode, using Courcelle’s representatives, the “partial satisfaction” of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{q}^{\rm out}
inside

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z.

Let

\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star}=\mathfrak{A}\setminus(\breve{C}\cup(Z\setminus\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)))\mbox{\penalty\ \penalty\ and\penalty\ \penalty\ }\mathfrak{A}^{\star}=\mathfrak{A}[\breve{C}\cup Z\cup V(F^{\star})].

Keep in mind that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z=I_{1}^{(d-r+1)}\setminus\breve{C}.

See
Figure 15
to get some intuition of the Gaifman graph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{\star}.

Verbally, the structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star}
is obtained from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
by removing from its universe the elements of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}
and the elements of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z\setminus\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z).
In other words, apart from the vertices in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star})
(that are in the universe of both
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{\star}
), the structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star}
corresponds to the part of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
that is “away” from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z,
while the structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{\star}
corresponds to the part of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
induced by the union of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F^{\star}).
Keep in mind that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star})\cap V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star})=\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star}).

Next, we will define two boundaried structures corresponding to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{\star},
whose boundary will be the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star}).

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}Z}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{{\mathfrak{A}}^{\star}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{\sf out}^{\star}}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{1}^{\star})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0.545,0,0.545}F^{\star}}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{1}^{\star})

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm in}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)

from

An ordering on the (common) boundary of the two structures.

We next claim that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star})=N_{G}(\breve{C}),
which directly implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}|\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star})|=h.

To see why
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star})=N_{G}(\breve{C}),
first observe that, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}\in{\sf cc}(G,X),
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G}(\breve{C})\subseteq X
and also notice that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm in}\cap N_{G}(\breve{C})=\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z).

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})=V({\bf a})\cap N_{G}(\breve{C})
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F^{\prime})=(X_{\rm out}\setminus V_{L}({\bf a}))\cap N_{G}(\breve{C}),
we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G}(\breve{C})=\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V_{L}({\bf a})\cup V(F^{\prime})=\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star}).

Therefore, we can consider an ordering
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}v_{1},\ldots,v_{h}
of the vertices in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star}).

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}^{\star}_{1}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{h})
and recall that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star})\cap V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star})=\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star}).

Now, consider the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structures
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}).

Notice that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})
are compatible
and that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})\oplus(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})=\mathfrak{A}.

To give a better understanding of the proof, we stress that our objective is
to find another “solution”
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}
that is “away” from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y,
the vertex set of the influence of the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{1}.
For this reason, we aim to use the equivalence of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}
to find another way to “look” at
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{\star},
by “changing” its boundary and finding this other
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}.

Adding
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F^{\star})
to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm in}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1}=X_{\rm in}\cup V(F^{\star}).

Now,
the fact that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\subseteq X_{\rm in}
implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{1}^{\star})\subseteq\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1}.

Also,
we set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star}:=R\cap(\breve{C}\cup Z).

Therefore, it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(R\setminus(\breve{C}\cup Z))\cup R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star}=R
and, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F^{\star})\subseteq X_{\rm out},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1}=X.

Separating
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},X)
into two boundaried structures.

We consider the structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},X).

We choose to include the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}
in the aforementioned structure (and not another
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid,
even if, due to
Subsection 9.1
, they would yield equivalent instances)
in
order to be able to “break”
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},X)
into two
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
-boundaried structures corresponding to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{\star},
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{\star}
to contain
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},
since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}\subseteq\breve{C}\cup Z\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}).

Thus, we have that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},X)=(\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star},R\setminus(\breve{C}\cup Z),\emptyset^{2q},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})\oplus(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}).

(10)

Also,
Subsection 7.1
implies that there is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textphi}\in{\sf rep}_{\texttau^{\prime}}^{(h)}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}),
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}=\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf R},{\sf X}\}\cup{\bf c},
such that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}.

Shifting from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{\star}
to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{(d,Z,L,F_{1})}.

Now, consider a graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{1}\in{\cal F}_{h-|\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)|}^{V_{L}({\bf a})}
that is isomorphic
6
6

6

In the rest of the proof of the claim, we will usually consider a subgraph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G,
or a structure with universe
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(G),
and isomorphic graphs/structures of them, and the latter will be “abstract” graphs/structures. For example, here we consider an “abstract” graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{1}
that is isomorphic to the graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\star}
that is a subgraph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G.
We will always use superscript “
⋆
” in order to denote the subgraphs/structures that are being given by the graph, while the lack of superscript reflects to the corresponding isomorphic “abstract” graphs/structures.
to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\star},
via a bijection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textxi:V(F_{1})\leftrightarrow V(F^{\star})
that maps every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a\in V_{L}({\bf a})
to itself.
Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{1}^{\prime}:=F_{1}\setminus V_{L}({\bf a}).

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal V}_{1}:=(\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z),V_{L}({\bf a}),V(F_{1}^{\prime}))
and observe that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal V}_{1}
is a nice 3-partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{1}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]\cup F_{1}.

Also, observe that the graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(K_{1}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]\cup F_{1})
has
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}Z}

A boundaried structure of bounded treewidth that satisfies
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textphi}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}_{1}
be the tuple obtained from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}
after replacing, in

Also, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}_{1}=\mathfrak{A}^{(d,Z,L,F_{1})}
(see
Figure 16
for a visualization of the Gaifman graph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{(d,Z,L,F_{1})}
).
Observe that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{1}\cap I_{1}^{(d)}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}_{1}).

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{1}:=(\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1}\setminus V(F^{\star}))\cup V(F_{1})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}:=R_{1}\cap(Z\setminus\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)).

Observe that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{1}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}_{1}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}_{1}),
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}=R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star}\setminus(\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup\breve{C}).

At this point, we stress that while
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{1}
is obtained from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1}

after replacing
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F^{\star})
with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F_{1}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}
is obtained from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star}
after removing all elements in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star})
that are in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}
and in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z).

We aim to show that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\bf H},\bar{\textphi})\in{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1},d,L,Z).

To show this, by the definition of
out
-
sig
it remains to prove that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},R_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}.

To prove the latter, first notice that, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{1}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\star}
are isomorphic, we have that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}_{1}[V({\bf b}_{1})],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{\star}[V({\bf b}_{1}^{\star})],
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star}[V({\bf b}_{1}^{\star})]
are (pairwise) isomorphic.
This implies that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}),
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})
are (pairwise) compatible.
We consider the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structures

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},R_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}\big).

These
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structures
are compatible.
We now show that they are also
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},h)
-equivalent, which will imply that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}\iff\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},R_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}.

Subclaim:

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},R_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}\big)

are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},h)
-equivalent.

Proof of Subclaim:

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}
be a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R^{\circ}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\in(2^{V(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ})})^{2q},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}^{\circ}
be an apex-tuple of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\circ}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}),
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}^{\circ}
be an apex-tuple of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{C}^{\circ}
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h,

such that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ},R^{\circ},{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ},{\bf a}^{\circ},X^{\circ},{\bf b}^{\circ})

is an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structure
that is compatible with the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structures
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},R_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}\big).

Our goal is to show that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ},R^{\circ},{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ},{\bf a}^{\circ},X^{\circ},{\bf b}^{\circ})\oplus\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\iff(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ},R^{\circ},{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ},{\bf a}^{\circ},X^{\circ},{\bf b}^{\circ})\oplus\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},R_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.

We set

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\star}:=(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ},{\bf b}^{\circ})\oplus(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}:=(\mathfrak{C}^{\circ},{\bf b}^{\circ})\oplus(\mathfrak{A}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}).

Equivalently, it suffices to prove that

(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},R^{\circ}\cup R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},{\bf a}^{\circ},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\iff(\mathfrak{B},R^{\circ}\cup R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},{\bf a}^{\circ},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.

By the definition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}
(see
Subsection 9.1
), it will be enough to show that

(i).

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1})\models\textbeta|_{{\sf star}_{X}}\iff(\mathfrak{B},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1})\models\textbeta|_{{\sf star}_{\sf X}},

(ii).

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{B}^{\star}}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\iff

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{B}},
and

(iii).

for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime}\in{\sf cc}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1})
that is not a subset of the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{B}^{\star}}
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},R^{\circ}\cup R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf a}^{0})[C^{\prime}]\models\breve{\textzeta}_{R}|_{\sf ap_{\bf c}}\wedge\textmu,

if and only if
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime}\in{\sf cc}(\mathfrak{B},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1})
that is not a subset of the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{B}}
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1},

it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{B},R^{\circ}\cup R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},{\bf a}^{0})[C^{\prime}]\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{\sf ap_{\bf c}}\wedge\textmu.

Note that, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\cap V(\mathfrak{A}_{1}^{\star}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{B}^{\star})\cap V(\mathfrak{B}).

Thus, item (ii) holds.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal C}^{\star}
be the set of all

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\in{\sf cc}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1})

that are not subsets of the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{B}^{\star}}
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1}^{\star}

and let

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal C}
be the set of all

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\in{\sf cc}(\mathfrak{B},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1})

that are not subsets of the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{B}}
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1}.

Observe that every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\in{\cal C}^{\star}

is a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z\setminus\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z).

Therefore, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{1}=(\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1}\setminus V(F^{\star}))\cup V(F_{1})

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}^{\star}}[Z]
is the same graph as
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}_{1}}[Z],
it holds that
every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\in{\cal C}
is also a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z\setminus\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)
and, moreover,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal C}={\cal C}^{\star}.

Also, observe that, since

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star}=R\cap(\breve{C}\cup Z)

while

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}=R_{1}\cap(Z\setminus\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)),

we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star}\cap(Z\setminus\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z))=R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}\cap(Z\setminus\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)).

Therefore, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\in{\cal C},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},R^{\circ}\cup R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star})[C]=(\mathfrak{B},R^{\circ}\cup R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}})[C].

Thus, item (iii) above holds.

It remains to prove that

(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1})\models\textbeta|_{{\sf star}_{\sf X}}\iff(\mathfrak{B},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1})\models\textbeta|_{{\sf star}_{\sf X}}.

To prove this, we will argue that the structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{B},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1})
is isomorphic to the structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1}).

To see why this holds, notice that
there is a bijection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textrho
between the universes of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1})
and of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{B},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1}),
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf x}\in{\sf R}^{{\sf star}_{X}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1})}
if and only if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf x}\in{\sf R}^{{\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{B},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1})}
for all
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf R}
of arity
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r\geq 1
and all
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf x}\in V({\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1}^{\star})).

This bijection is the identity function for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}x\in X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1}
and every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}v\in V({\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1}))
that corresponds to a set in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf cc}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1})\setminus{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{B}^{\star}},{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1}).

The element
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}v\in V({\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1}))
that corresponds to the unique element in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{B}^{\star}},{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1})
is mapped to the element in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\sf star}(\mathfrak{B},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1}))
that corresponds to the unique element in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{B}},{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1}).

To get some intuition why this bijection satisfies the above condition, notice that
if we “contract” the vertex set in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{B}^{\star}},{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1})
and the vertex set in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{B}},{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1})

to two single vertices
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}v^{\star}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}v,
respectively, then
the neighbors of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}v^{\star}
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1}
and the neighbors of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}v
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1}
induce isomorphic graphs in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{B}^{\star}}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{B}},
respectively. The latter holds due to the fact that if a vertex of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1}
is connected with a vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{B}^{\star}},{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1}),
then
the corresponding vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1}
is also connected with a vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{B}},{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1}).

Therefore,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},R^{\circ}\cup R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},{\bf a}^{\circ},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\iff(\mathfrak{B},R^{\circ}\cup R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},{\bf a}^{\circ},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}
and the subclaim follows.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\diamond

Since by the above subclaim,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},R_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}\big)

are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},h)
-equivalent,
it follows that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}\iff\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},R_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}.

(11)

Therefore, we conclude that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{1},{\bf b}_{1},
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{1}

certify that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\bf H},\bar{\textphi})\in{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1},d,L,Z).

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1},d,L,Z)={\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}(\mathfrak{K}_{2},R_{2},d,L,Z^{\prime}),
we have
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\bf H},\bar{\textphi})\in{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}(\mathfrak{K}_{2},R_{2},d,L,Z^{\prime}).

Thus,

(a).

there is an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{2}\in{\cal F}_{h-|\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})|}^{V_{L}({\bf a})}
such that if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf H}=(H,{\cal U})
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal V}_{2}=(\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime}),V_{L}({\bf a}),V(F_{2})\setminus V_{L}({\bf a})),

then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal V}_{2}
is a nice 3-partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{2}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]\cup F_{2}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{2}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]\cup F_{2}
is strongly isomorphic to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}H
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\cal V}_{2},{\cal U})
and

(b).

there is an ordering
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}_{2}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\cup V(F_{2})

and an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{2}\subseteq Z^{\prime}\cup V(F_{2}),

such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\cup V(F_{2})\subseteq\tilde{X}_{2}
and,
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}}=(Z^{\prime}\setminus\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime}))\cap R_{2},

then

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{2}^{(d,Z^{\prime},L,F_{2})},R_{2}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}.

Observe that,
since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{1}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]\cup F_{1}

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{2}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]\cup F_{2}

are strongly isomorphic to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}H
with respect to

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\cal V}_{1},{\cal U})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\cal V}_{2},{\cal U}),
respectively,
we also have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{1}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]\cup F_{1}

is strongly isomorphic to

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{2}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]\cup F_{2}

with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\cal V}_{1},{\cal V}_{2}).

We now set

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{2}^{\prime}=F_{2}\setminus V_{L}({\bf a})

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}_{2}=\mathfrak{A}_{2}^{(d,Z^{\prime},L,F_{2})}
(see
Figure 17
for an example of its Gaifman graph).

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{{\mathfrak{A}}_{2}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0.545,0,0.545}F_{2}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{2})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(d)}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{2}^{\prime}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}Z^{\prime}}

Figure 17
:
The Gaifman graph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\mathfrak{A}_{2}}.

Notice that the fact that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{1}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]\cup F_{1}

is strongly isomorphic to

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{2}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]\cup F_{2}

with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\cal V}_{1},{\cal V}_{2})

implies that the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structures

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R_{2}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}\big)

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},R_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}\big)

are compatible.
Thus, given that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R_{2}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}\big)\models\bar{\textphi},
we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R_{2}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}\big)
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},R_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}\big)
are

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},h)
-equivalent.
Therefore,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},R_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}\iff\big(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R_{2}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}.

(12)

At this point, to give some intuition, we underline that even if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R_{2}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}_{1},R_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1})
are

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},h)
-equivalent, we did not yet provide
a boundaried structure that is a
substructure
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},\varnothing^{l},X)
and that
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},h)
-equivalent to

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}).

To find such a substructure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star})
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},\varnothing^{l},X),

we have to “shift” from

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R_{2}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2})

to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}_{2}^{\star},R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}),

by replacing
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F_{2})
with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F^{\star}),
and “extending”
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{2}^{\prime}
to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star}

so to contain all vertices in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star}\setminus Y.

This substructure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star})
will replace
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})
in (
10
),
thus providing a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}\subseteq Z^{\prime}
such that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\subseteq X^{\prime}
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},X)\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\iff(\mathfrak{A},R\setminus Y,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.

Defining a substructure of the initial structure with a different boundary.

Let us now define this substructure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star})
from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R_{2}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}\big).

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}
to be
the tuple obtained from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}_{2}
after replacing each
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}v\in V(F_{2})
with the corresponding
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}u\in V(F^{\star}).
See
Figure 18
for an example.
We stress that the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star})
of
Figure 18

can also be defined as the one obtained from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})
of
Figure 15
after replacing
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)
with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})
in the boundary.
Also, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star}=R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star}\setminus Y,
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2}=(\tilde{X}_{2}\setminus V(F_{2}))\cup V(F^{\star}).

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{{\mathfrak{A}}^{\star}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0.545,0,0.545}F^{\star}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{2}^{\star})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}Z^{\prime}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}^{\prime}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}

Figure 18
:
The Gaifman graph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\mathfrak{A}^{\star}},
when adding
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})
to the boundary. The set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}
is the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{2}\setminus V(F^{\star})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}^{\prime}
is the privileged connected component of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}^{\star}}
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}.

For the tuple
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star})
to be an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structure, we need to show that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{2}^{\star})\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}).

Notice that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),
since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star}=R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star}\setminus Y
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}).

To show that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{2}^{\star})\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),
we first notice that, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}
respects
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(d)}\cap X=\emptyset,
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(d)}\subseteq\breve{C}.

Therefore, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z^{\prime}\subseteq I_{2}^{(d-r+1)},
we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z^{\prime}\subseteq\breve{C}.

The latter implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})
is a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}).

By the definition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}
and since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{2})=\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\cup V(F_{2}),
we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{2}^{\star})=\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\cup V(F^{\star}).

Hence, given that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F^{\star})\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{2}^{\star})\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}).

Also, observe that the fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(d)}\subseteq\breve{C}
implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),
while
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),
since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2}=(\tilde{X}_{2}\setminus V(F_{2}))\cup V(F^{\star}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{2}\setminus V(F_{2})\subseteq Z^{\prime},
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z^{\prime}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}).

All considered boundaried structures are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},h)
-equivalent.

As a next step, we argue that the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structures

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R_{2}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}),

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})
are (pairwise) compatible.
To see why this holds, notice that, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{1}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]\cup F_{1}

is strongly isomorphic to

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{2}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\cup V_{L}({\bf a})]\cup F_{2}

with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\cal V}_{1},{\cal V}_{2}),
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{1}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{2}
are isomorphic. This, together with the fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{1}
is isomorphic to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\star},
implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{2},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{1},
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\star}
are pairwise isomorphic graphs.
Therefore,
the structures
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{\star}[V({\bf b}_{2}^{\star})],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}_{2}[V({\bf b}_{2})],
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{\star}[V({\bf b}_{1}^{\star})]
are (pairwise) isomorphic.

By following the exactly symmetric arguments as in the proof of the subclaim above,
it is easy to show that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R_{2}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2})
are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},h)
-equivalent. This implies that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}\iff\big(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R_{2}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}.

(13)

Therefore, combining (
11
), (
12
), and (
13
), we conclude that
the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structures
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}\big)

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)

are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},h)
-equivalent.
Recall that, by (
10
),

(\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star},R\setminus(\breve{C}\cup Z),\emptyset^{2q},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})\oplus(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})=(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},X)

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},X)\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.

Since the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structures
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}\big)

are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},h)
-equivalent,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle(\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star},R\setminus(\breve{C}\cup Z),\emptyset^{2q},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})\oplus\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}\big)\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.

(14)

Another way to put a boundary in the initial structure.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}:=\tilde{X}_{2}^{\star}.

To conclude the proof of
Claim 1
, it remains to prove that

(\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star},R\setminus(\breve{C}\cup Z),\emptyset^{2q},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})\oplus\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}\big)=(\mathfrak{A},R\setminus Y,{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}).

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}Z}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{1}^{\star})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm in}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{2}^{\star})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\prime}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}Z^{\prime}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}^{\prime}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}

Figure 19
:
Left: The Gaifman graph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star},R\setminus(\breve{C}\cup Z),\emptyset^{2q},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})\oplus(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}).

Right: The Gaifman graph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star},R\setminus(\breve{C}\cup Z),\emptyset^{2q},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})\oplus\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}\big).

To see why this holds, note that the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structures
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star},R\setminus(\breve{C}\cup Z),\emptyset^{2q},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}\big)
are compatible and that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\setminus(\breve{C}\cup Z)\cup R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star}=R\setminus(\breve{C}\cup Z)\cup(R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star}\setminus Y)=R\setminus Y
(the latter equality holds since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\setminus(\breve{C}\cup Z)\cup R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star}=R,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y=V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1}}(W^{\bullet}))\subseteq I_{1}^{(d-r+1)},
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{1}^{(d-r+1)}\subseteq\breve{C}\cup Z
).
See
Figure 19
for an example of how
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)
is “transformed” to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}\big).

Finally, we have that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},X)\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\iff(\mathfrak{A},R\setminus Y,{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.

To conclude the proof of
Claim 1
, it remains to prove that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V\subseteq Y

that is also a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\breve{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime}}(\overline{W}),
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\overline{W}
is the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(j^{\prime}-2)
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{1}

,

(\mathfrak{A},R\setminus Y,{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\iff(\mathfrak{A}\setminus V,R\setminus Y,{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V\subseteq Y
that is also a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\breve{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime}}(\overline{W})),
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\overline{W}
is the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(j^{\prime}-2)
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{1}.

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y\subseteq I_{1}^{(w)},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\cap Y=\emptyset,
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)}\subseteq I_{2}^{(2)},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime\prime}\in{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{A}},{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\iff C^{\prime\prime}\setminus V\in{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V,{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})
and, if

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal C}
(resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal C}^{\prime}
) is the set of all
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime\prime}\in{\sf cc}(G_{\mathfrak{A}},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})
(resp. all
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime\prime}\in{\sf cc}(G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V,X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})
)
that are not in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{A}},{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})
(resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V,{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})
), then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal C}={\cal C}^{\prime}.

Therefore, to show that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R\setminus Y,{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\iff(\mathfrak{A}\setminus V,R\setminus Y,{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},
it now suffices to prove
that

(\mathfrak{A},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\models\textbeta|_{{\sf star}_{\sf X}}\iff(\mathfrak{A}\setminus V,X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\models\textbeta|_{{\sf star}_{\sf X}}.

Equivalently,
we want to prove that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{A},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\models\textbeta\iff{\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{A}\setminus V,X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\models\textbeta.

Recall that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y=V({\sf compass}_{\breve{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime}}(\breve{W}^{\prime})),
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\breve{W}^{\prime},\breve{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime})
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{W}
-tilt of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{W}
is the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{1}.

Note that, by the definition of a flatness pair and since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V\subseteq V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\breve{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime}}(\overline{W})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\overline{W}
is the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(j^{\prime}-2)
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{1},

no vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V
is adjacent to a vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus(Y\cup V({\bf a})).

Also, the fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\cap Y=\emptyset

implies that every vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}
is either in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus Y
or in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf a}).

Therefore, if a vertex of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V
is adjacent to a vertex
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}u
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime},
then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}u\in V({\bf a}).

We will prove that every vertex
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}u
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}
that is adjacent, in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}},
to a vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V
is also adjacent, in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}},
to a vertex in

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\sf compass}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W))\setminus V.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}u
be a vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}
that is adjacent, in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}},
to a vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V.

As observed above,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}u\in V({\bf a}).

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
be a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
-canonical partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a}).

Since by the hypothesis of the lemma
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf bid}_{(W,\mathfrak{R})}(N_{G_{\mathfrak{A}}}(V({\bf a})))\geq(g+2)^{2}+1,

we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}u
is adjacent to at least
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(g+2)^{2}+1
internal bags of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}.

Notice that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V
is a subset of the union of the vertex sets of all internal bags of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
that intersect the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(g+2)
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{1}.

These bags are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(g+2)^{2}
many.
This, in turn, implies that, since the vertex
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}u
is adjacent to at least
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(g+2)^{2}+1
internal bags of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a
is adjacent to a vertex
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}v

in the vertex set of an internal bag of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
that
is disjoint from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V.

By observing that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}v\in V({\sf compass}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W))\setminus V,

we conclude that every vertex
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}u\in(X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\cap V({\bf a})
is adjacent to a vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\sf compass}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W))\setminus V.

This implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{A},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})={\sf star}_{\sf X}(\mathfrak{A}\setminus V,X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}).

Claim 1
follows.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\diamond

Following
Claim 1
, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}\subseteq Z^{\prime}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\subseteq X^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},X)\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\iff(\mathfrak{A},R\setminus Y,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.

Observe that since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}\subseteq I_{1}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{1}^{(d\cdot r)}\cap(X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})=\emptyset,
we have that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}\subseteq G\setminus(X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}).

Thus, there is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}^{\prime}\in{\sf cc}(G,X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})
that respects
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}.

Therefore, by
Subsection 7.3
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\{\breve{C}^{\prime}\}={\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}).

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime}
be the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}
