and keep in mind that if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w=\circ,
then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime}=\breve{C}^{\prime},
while, if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w=\bullet,
then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime}=V(G)\setminus(X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}^{\prime}\subseteq C^{\prime}.

Also, since every vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf a})
is adjacent, in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G,
to at least
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
internal bags of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q},

we have that
every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a\in V({\bf a})
is either in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})

or belongs to both
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}^{\prime}.

Therefore,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}\cap\breve{C}={\bf a}\cap\breve{C}^{\prime}={\bf a}\cap C={\bf a}\cap C^{\prime}.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}^{\prime}:={\bf a}\cap\breve{C}.

We aim to prove the following:

Claim 2.

It holds that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C])\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}\iff{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R\setminus Y,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C^{\prime}])\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}.

Proof of
Claim 2
:

We will only prove that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C])\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}\implies{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R\setminus Y,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C^{\prime}])\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R},

since the other implication is trivial.
Suppose that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C])\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}.

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}
is a Boolean combination of the basic local sentences
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{1},\ldots,\breve{\textzeta}_{p},
there is a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}J\subseteq[p]
such that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j\in J
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C])\models\breve{\textzeta}_{j}
and for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j\notin J
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C])\models\neg\breve{\textzeta}_{j}.

We will show that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j\in J
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R\setminus Y,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C^{\prime}])\models\breve{\textzeta}_{j}
and that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j\notin J
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R\setminus Y,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C^{\prime}])\models\neg\breve{\textzeta}_{j}.

Therefore, we distinguish two cases.

Case 1:

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j\in J.

We aim to prove that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C])\models\breve{\textzeta}_{j}\iff{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R\setminus Y,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C^{\prime}])\models\breve{\textzeta}_{j}.

Suppose that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C])\models\breve{\textzeta}_{j}.

Recall that the constant-projection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttau\cup\{R\})^{\bf c}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttau\cup\{R\}\cup{\bf c}),
i.e.,
the vocabulary of the structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C]),
contains every unary relation symbol in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttau\cup\{R\}\cup{\bf c})

and note that in the structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C]),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R
is interpreted as
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\cap C.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{C}:=R\cap C,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{B},R_{C}):={\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C]),

and keep in mind that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\langle\bf c\rangle}
-structure.
Since the Gaifman graphs of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}
and of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{B},R_{C})
are the same, in the rest of the proof we will use
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{B}}
to denote both of them.
Also, to get some intuition, notice that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{B}}
is obtained from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G[C]
after removing some edges (namely, the edges of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G[C]
that connect the vertices in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf a}^{\prime})
with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\setminus V({\bf a}^{\prime})
).

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{j}
is a basic local sentence with parameters
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{j}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell_{j},

we have that

(\mathfrak{B},R_{C})\models\breve{\textzeta}_{j}\iff\exists X_{j}\subseteq R_{C}\mbox{\penalty\ that is $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j},r_{j})$-scattered in $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}$ and $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}}\textpsi_{j}(x)$}.

We prove the following, which intuitively states that, given the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j},
we can find another set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\prime}
that “behaves” in the same way as
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}
but also “avoids” some inner part of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{2}^{\bf a}.

Subclaim:
There exists a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t\in[d-\frac{r}{2}+2\hat{r}+1,d-\hat{r}]
and a set

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\prime}
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j},r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}\subseteq R_{C},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}}\textpsi_{j}(x)\iff\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}^{\prime}}\textpsi_{j}(x),
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\prime}\cap I_{2}^{(t)}=\emptyset.

Proof of Subclaim:

Our goal is to find a flatness pair, say
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{3},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{3}),
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent to

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{2},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{2}),
and a proper “buffer”
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t
so as to replace the part of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}
that is in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(t)}
to an “equivalent” one that is inside
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{3}^{(t)}.

For this replacement to be “safe”, we first have to demand that the the influence of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{3},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{3}),
i.e., the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{3}^{(w)},
is disjoint from both the modulator
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}
and the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}.

Recall that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime\prime}
is a collection of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{\ell}+2
flatness pairs of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a})
that are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1})
and the vertex sets of their influences are disjoint from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}.

Therefore, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}
has size at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{\ell},
there exists a flatness pair in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime\prime}\setminus\{(\tilde{W}_{2},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{2})\},
say
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{3},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{3}),
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{3}^{(w)}\cap(X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}\cup X_{j})=\emptyset.

We now focus on the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(d)}\setminus I_{2}^{(d-r+1)}.

Recall that for the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}\subseteq Z^{\prime}\subseteq I_{2}^{(d-r)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cap I_{2}^{(w)}=\emptyset.

Therefore,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(d)}\setminus I_{2}^{(d-r+1)}
does not intersect the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}.

Since

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r=2\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)\cdot\hat{r}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}|X_{j}|\leq\hat{\ell},

there exists a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t\in[d-\frac{r}{2}+2\hat{r}+1,d-\hat{r}]

such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}
does not intersect
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(t)}\setminus I_{2}^{(t-\hat{r}+1)}.

Intuitively, we partition the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r
layers of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{2}
that are in

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(d)}\setminus I_{2}^{(d-r+1)}
into two parts,
the first
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r/2
layers and the second
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r/2
layers,
and then we find some layer among
the “
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{r}
-central”
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\hat{\ell}+1)\hat{r}
layers of the second part.
This layer together with its preceding
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{r}-1
layers define a “buffer” of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{r}
that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}
“avoids” - that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(t)}\setminus I_{2}^{(t-\hat{r}+1)}.

Notice that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(t)}\setminus I_{2}^{(t-\hat{r}+1)}
is a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(d)}\setminus I_{2}^{(d-r+1)}

and therefore
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(t)}\setminus I_{2}^{(t-\hat{r}+1)}
intersects neither
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}
nor
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\star}:=X_{j}\cap I_{2}^{(t-\hat{r}+1)}

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y_{j}\subseteq[\ell_{j}]
to be the set of indices of the vertices in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\star}.

Notice that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\star}\subseteq R_{2},
given that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\star}=X_{j}\cap I_{2}^{(t-\hat{r}+1)}\subseteq R_{C}\cap I_{2}^{(t-\hat{r}+1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{C}\cap I_{2}^{(t-\hat{r}+1)}\subseteq R_{2}.

Therefore, since

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\star}=X_{j}\cap I_{2}^{(t-\hat{r}+1)},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textpsi_{j}(x)
is an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{j}
-local formula (where “
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{j}
-local” refers to distances in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{B}}
),
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{r}\geq r_{j},
we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}^{\star}}\textpsi_{j}(x)\iff\mathfrak{B}[I_{2}^{(t)}]\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}^{\star}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

To sum up, we observe that, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)}\subseteq I_{2}^{(t)},
we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(K_{2}^{\bf a}[I_{2}^{(t)}],{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},\emptyset)=\{I_{2}^{(t)}\}
and also the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\star}
is a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(t-\hat{r}+1)}\cap R_{2}
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(|Y_{j}|,r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}[I_{2}^{(t)}]
(since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(|Y_{j}|,r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}
) and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}^{\star}}\textpsi_{j}(x)\iff\mathfrak{B}[I_{2}^{(t)}]\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}^{\star}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

(15)

Also, notice that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}(\mathfrak{A},{\bf a}^{\prime})[I_{2}^{(t)}]=\mathfrak{B}[I_{2}^{(t)}].

Using the fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{2},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{2})
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{3},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{3}),

we now aim to find a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{j}
that is an “equivalent” (in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{3}^{(t)}
) set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\star}.

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{2},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{2})
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{3},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{3}),
we have that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf in\mbox{-}sig}(\mathfrak{K}_{2},R_{2},t^{\prime},L,\emptyset)={\sf in\mbox{-}sig}(\mathfrak{K}_{3},R_{3},t^{\prime},L,\emptyset),

for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t^{\prime}\in[w].

Therefore, we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf in\mbox{-}sig}(\mathfrak{K}_{2},R_{2},t,L,\emptyset)={\sf in\mbox{-}sig}(\mathfrak{K}_{3},R_{3},t,L,\emptyset)
for the particular value
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t
given above.
This implies that there exists a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{C}\in{\sf pr}(K_{3}^{\bf a}[I_{3}^{(t)}],{\bf W}_{q}^{(3)},\emptyset)
and a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X_{j}}\subseteq I_{3}^{(t-\hat{r}+1)}\cap R_{3}
such that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X_{j}}
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(|Y_{j}|,r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}[I_{3}^{(t)}]
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}(\mathfrak{A},{\bf a}^{\prime})[I_{2}^{(t)}]\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}^{\star}}\textpsi_{j}(x)\iff{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}(\mathfrak{A},{\bf a}^{\prime})[\hat{C}]\models\bigwedge_{x\in\tilde{X}_{j}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

Observe that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}(\mathfrak{A},{\bf a}^{\prime})[\hat{C}]=\mathfrak{B}[\hat{C}]
and that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(K_{3}^{\bf a}[I_{3}^{(t)}],{\bf W}_{q}^{(3)},\emptyset)=\{I_{3}^{(t)}\}.

Thus,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}[I_{2}^{(t)}]\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}^{\star}}\textpsi_{j}(x)\iff\mathfrak{B}[I_{3}^{(t)}]\models\bigwedge_{x\in\tilde{X}_{j}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

(16)

Given that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X\cap I_{3}^{(w)}=\emptyset,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}\in{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{q}^{(3)},X),
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{q}^{(3)}\subseteq I_{3}^{(w)},

we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{3}^{(w)}\subseteq\breve{C}\subseteq C.

We stress that the above holds, no matter which
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
we consider.
Also, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X_{j}}\subseteq I_{3}^{(t-\hat{r}+1)}\subseteq I_{3}^{(w-\hat{r})},
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}x\in\tilde{X_{j}}
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G_{\mathfrak{B}}}^{(\leq\hat{r})}(x)\subseteq C.

Thus, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textpsi_{j}({\sf x})
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{j}
-local, it follows that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}[I_{3}^{(t)}]\models\bigwedge_{x\in\tilde{X}_{j}}\textpsi_{j}(x)\iff\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in\tilde{X}_{j}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

(17)

We now consider the set

{X}^{\prime}_{j}:=\left(X_{j}\setminus X_{j}^{\star}\right)\cup\tilde{X_{j}}.

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{3}^{(w)}\cap X_{j}=\emptyset
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{r}\geq r_{j},
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}x\in X_{j},
and thus, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}x\in X_{j}\setminus X_{j}^{\star},
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G_{\mathfrak{B}}}^{(\leq r_{j})}(x)\cap I_{3}^{(w-\hat{r}+1)}=\emptyset.

Also, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t\leq w-\hat{r}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X_{j}}\subseteq I_{3}^{(t-\hat{r}+1)},
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}x\in\tilde{X_{j}}
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G_{\mathfrak{B}}}^{(\leq r_{j})}(x)\subseteq I_{3}^{(w-\hat{r}+1)}.

Thus, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}x\in X_{j}\setminus X_{j}^{\star}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}x^{\prime}\in\tilde{X_{j}}
we have that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G_{\mathfrak{B}}}^{(\leq r_{j})}(x)\cap N_{G_{\mathfrak{B}}}^{(\leq r_{j})}(x^{\prime})=\emptyset.

The latter, together with the fact that the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}\setminus X_{j}^{\star}
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j}-|Y_{j}|,r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X_{j}}
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(|Y_{j}|,r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}[I_{3}^{(t)}],

implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{X}^{\prime}_{j}
is an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j},r_{j})
-scattered set in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}.

Moreover, by definition, we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{X}^{\prime}_{j}\subseteq R_{C}\cup R_{3}=R_{C}
(the latter equality holds since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{3}^{(w)}\subseteq C
) and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{X}^{\prime}_{j}
does not intersect
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(t)},
while, by (
15
), (
16
), and (
17
), we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}}\textpsi_{j}(x)\iff\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}^{\prime}}\textpsi_{j}(x).
The subclaim follows.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\diamond

Following the above subclaim, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t\in[d-\frac{r}{2}+2\hat{r}+1,d-\hat{r}]
and let

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\prime}
be a set that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j},r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq R_{C},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}}\textpsi_{j}(x)\iff\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}^{\prime}}\textpsi_{j}(x),
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\prime}\cap I_{2}^{(t)}=\emptyset.

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r=2\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)\cdot\hat{r}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}|{X}^{\prime}_{j}|\leq\hat{\ell},
there exists a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t^{\prime}\in[d-r+2\hat{r}+1,d-\frac{r}{2}-\hat{r}]
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{X}^{\prime}_{j}
does not intersect
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{1}^{(t^{\prime})}\setminus I_{1}^{(t^{\prime}-\hat{r}+1)}.

Intuitively, here, we partition the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r
layers of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{1}
that are in

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{1}^{(d)}\setminus I_{1}^{(d-r+1)}
into two parts,
the first
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r/2
layers and the second
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r/2
layers,
and then we find some layer among
the “
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{r}
-central”
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\hat{\ell}+1)\hat{r}
layers of the
first
part.
This layer together with its preceding
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{r}-1
layers define a “buffer” of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{r}
that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\prime}
“avoids” - that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{1}^{(t^{\prime})}\setminus I_{1}^{(t^{\prime}-\hat{r}+1)}.

Now, consider the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}U_{1}:={X}^{\prime}_{j}\cap(I_{1}^{(t^{\prime}-\hat{r}+1)}\setminus Z).

Observe that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}U_{1}\subseteq R_{1}
and therefore
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}U_{1}\subseteq(I_{1}^{(t^{\prime}-\hat{r}+1)}\setminus Z)\cap R_{1}.

Recall that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y=I_{1}^{(\hat{r})}

and notice that, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({X}^{\prime}_{j}\setminus U_{1})\cap I_{1}^{(t^{\prime})}=\emptyset
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t^{\prime}>\hat{r},

it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{X}^{\prime}_{j}\setminus U_{1}\subseteq R\setminus Y.

We now observe that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},X_{\rm out}\cup Z)={\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{{(1)}},X),

due to the fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\subseteq X.

Recall that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{{(1)}},X)=\{\breve{C}\}.

Τhus,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},X_{\rm out}\cup Z)=\{\breve{C}\}.

Also, recall that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cap I_{1}^{(d)}=\emptyset.

Therefore,
the fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}\subseteq I_{1}^{(d)}
implies that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(K_{1}^{\bf a}[I_{1}^{(d)}],{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},Z)
contains a unique element,
say
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{1}.

Observe that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{1}^{(t^{\prime})}\setminus Z\subseteq C_{1}.

Also, note that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{1}\subseteq\breve{C}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq[\ell_{j}]
be the set of the indices of the vertices of

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{X}^{\prime}_{j}
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}U_{1}.

Given that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}U_{1}={X}^{\prime}_{j}\cap(I_{1}^{(t^{\prime}-\hat{r}+1)}\setminus Z)
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{X}^{\prime}_{j}
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j},r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B},

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}^{\prime}}\textpsi_{j}(x),

we get that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}U_{1}
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(|Y_{j}^{\prime}|,r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}[I_{1}^{(t^{\prime})}\setminus Z]

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in U_{1}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

At this point, observe that,
since the formula
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textpsi_{j}({\sf x})
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{j}
-local,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}U_{1}={X}^{\prime}_{j}\cap(I_{1}^{(t^{\prime}-\hat{r}+1)}\setminus Z),

where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{r}\geq r_{j}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t^{\prime}\leq d-\frac{r}{2}-\hat{r},
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}x\in U_{1}
we have that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G_{\mathfrak{B}}}^{(\leq r_{j})}(x)\subseteq I_{1}^{(d)}\setminus Z.

Thus, the fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{1}^{(t^{\prime})}\setminus Z\subseteq C_{1}
implies that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in U_{1}}\textpsi_{j}(x)\iff\mathfrak{B}[C_{1}]\models\bigwedge_{x\in U_{1}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

(18)

Also, note that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}(\mathfrak{A},{\bf a}^{\prime})[C_{1}]=\mathfrak{B}[C_{1}].

As we mentioned before,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf in\mbox{-}sig}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1},d,L,Z)={\sf in\mbox{-}sig}(\mathfrak{K}_{2},R_{2},d,L,Z^{\prime}).

This implies the existence of a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{2}\in{\sf pr}(K_{2}^{\bf a}[I_{2}^{(d)}],{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},Z^{\prime})
and a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}U_{2}\subseteq(I_{2}^{(t^{\prime}-\hat{r})}\setminus Z^{\prime})\cap R_{2}\subseteq R\setminus Y

such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}U_{2}
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(|Y_{j}^{\prime}|,r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}[I_{2}^{(t^{\prime})}\setminus Z^{\prime}]
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}[C_{1}]\models\bigwedge_{x\in U_{1}}\textpsi_{j}(x)\iff{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}(\mathfrak{A},{\bf a}^{\prime})[C_{2}]\models\bigwedge_{x\in U_{2}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\mathfrak{B}}:={\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}(\mathfrak{A},{\bf a}^{\prime})[C_{2}].

Therefore, we have that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}[C_{1}]\models\bigwedge_{x\in U_{1}}\textpsi_{j}(x)\iff\tilde{\mathfrak{B}}\models\bigwedge_{x\in U_{2}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

(19)

By (
18
) and (
19
), we derive that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in U_{1}}\textpsi_{j}(x)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\iff

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{B}}\models\bigwedge_{x\in U_{2}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

(20)

We now observe that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},X_{\rm out}\cup Z^{\prime})={\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{{(1)}},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}).

To see this, notice that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{{(1)}},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})={\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},X_{\rm out}\cup Z^{\prime})={\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}),
due to the fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\subseteq X^{\prime}.

Τhus,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},X_{\rm out}\cup Z^{\prime})=\{\breve{C}^{\prime}\}.

Recall that for the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime}
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}\subseteq Z^{\prime}\subseteq I_{2}^{(d-r)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cap I_{2}^{(w)}=\emptyset.

Since

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{2}\in{\sf pr}(K_{2}^{\bf a}[I_{2}^{(d)}],{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},Z^{\prime}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{\rm out}\cap I_{2}^{(w)}=\emptyset,

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)}\subseteq I_{2}^{(d)},

it holds that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(t^{\prime})}\setminus Z^{\prime}\subseteq C_{2}
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{2}\subseteq\breve{C}^{\prime}\subseteq C^{\prime}.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}:=\mathfrak{A}[C^{\prime}]

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{C^{\prime}}:=R\cap{C^{\prime}}

and we observe that, by construction,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf a}\cap C^{\prime})=V({\bf a}\cap C).

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}U_{2}
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(|Y_{j}^{\prime}|,r_{j})
-scattered in

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}[I_{2}^{(t^{\prime})}\setminus Z^{\prime}],
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}U_{2}\subseteq I_{2}^{(t^{\prime}-\hat{r}+1)}\setminus Z^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t^{\prime}<w-\hat{r},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}U_{2}
is also
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(|Y_{j}^{\prime}|,r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}.

Moreover, the formula
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textpsi_{j}({\sf x})
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{j}
-local, so

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{B}}\models\bigwedge_{x\in U_{2}}\textpsi_{j}(x)\iff\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}\models\bigwedge_{x\in U_{2}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

(21)

Therefore, by (
20
) and (
21
), it follows that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in U_{1}}\textpsi_{j}(x)\iff\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}\models\bigwedge_{x\in U_{2}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

Consider the set

X_{j}^{\bullet}:=({X}^{\prime}_{j}\setminus U_{1})\cup U_{2}.

Notice that since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{X}^{\prime}_{j}\setminus U_{1}\subseteq C

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{X}^{\prime}_{j}\setminus U_{1}
does not intersect neither

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(d-r+1)}
(where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X^{\prime}
lies),
nor
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{1}^{(d-r+1)}\subseteq I_{1}^{(t^{\prime})}
(where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z
lies),
it follows that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{X}^{\prime}_{j}\setminus U_{1}\subseteq C\cap C^{\prime}.

This implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{X}^{\prime}_{j}\setminus U_{1}
is an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j}-|Y_{j}^{\prime}|,r_{j})
-scattered set in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}

and an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j}-|Y_{j}^{\prime}|,r_{j})
-scattered set in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}.

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}U_{2}\subseteq I_{2}^{(t^{\prime}-\hat{r}+1)}\setminus Z^{\prime},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{X}^{\prime}_{j}\cap I_{2}^{(t)}=\emptyset,

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t^{\prime}\leq t-2\hat{r},

we have that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}x\in{X}^{\prime}_{j}\setminus U_{1}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}x^{\prime}\in U_{2}
it holds that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G_{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}}}^{(\leq r_{j})}(x)\cap N_{G_{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}}}^{(\leq r_{j})}(x^{\prime})=\emptyset.

The latter, together with the fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{X}^{\prime}_{j}\setminus U_{1}
is an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j}-|Y_{j}^{\prime}|,r_{j})
-scattered set in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}U_{2}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(|Y_{j}^{\prime}|,r_{j})
-scattered set in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime},

implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\bullet}
is an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j},r_{j})
-scattered set in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}.

Also, notice that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\bullet}\subseteq R_{C^{\prime}}\setminus Y.

Furthermore, since the formula
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textpsi_{j}({\sf x})
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{j}
-local, it follows that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}}\textpsi_{j}(x)\iff\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}^{\bullet}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

Thus, assuming that there is a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}\subseteq R_{C}
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j},r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}}\textpsi_{j}(x),
we proved that there is a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\bullet}\subseteq R_{C^{\prime}}\setminus Y\subseteq R\setminus Y
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j},r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}^{\bullet}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

To conclude Case 1, notice that we can prove the inverse implication,
i.e., by assuming the existence of a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\bullet}\subseteq R_{C^{\prime}}\setminus Y\subseteq R\setminus Y
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j},r_{j})
-scattered
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}^{\bullet}}\textpsi_{j}(x)

and, by using
the same arguments as above
(replacing
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1})
with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{2},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{2}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z
with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R
with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\setminus Y
),
we can prove the existence of a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}\subseteq R
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j},r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

Case 2:

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j\notin J.

We aim to prove that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C])\models\neg\breve{\textzeta}_{j}\iff{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R\setminus Y,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C^{\prime}])\models\neg\breve{\textzeta}_{j}.

In other words, we show that for every set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}\subseteq R\cap C
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j},r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}\models\neg\textpsi_{j}(x),
for some
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}x\in X_{j}
if and only if for every set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\prime}\subseteq(R\setminus Y)\cap C^{\prime}
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j},r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}\models\neg\textpsi_{j}(x),
for some
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}x\in X_{j}^{\prime}.

In Case 1, we showed that there is a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}\subseteq R\cap C
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j},r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}}\textpsi_{j}(x)
if and only if there is a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{j}^{\bullet}\subseteq(R\cap C^{\prime})\setminus Y\subseteq R\setminus Y
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\ell_{j},r_{j})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}\models\bigwedge_{x\in X_{j}^{\bullet}}\textpsi_{j}(x).

This directly implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C])\models\neg\breve{\textzeta}_{j}\iff{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R\setminus Y,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C^{\prime}])\models\neg\breve{\textzeta}_{j}.

This concludes Case 2 and completes the proof of
Claim 2
.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\diamond

Claim 3.

It holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}[C]\models\textmu\iff\mathfrak{A}[C^{\prime}]\models\textmu.

Proof of
Claim 3
:
Observe that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}\models\textmu\iff G_{\mathfrak{A}}\in{\sf excl}(\{K_{{\bf hw}(\texttheta)}\}).

Also, observe that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\setminus D=C^{\prime}\setminus D.

If
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y=V(\mathfrak{A})\setminus C
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y^{\prime}=V(\mathfrak{A})\setminus C^{\prime},
then note that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y\setminus D=Y^{\prime}\setminus D
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y
intersects at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
bags of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}.

Thus, by assumption,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus Y\in{\sf excl}(\{K_{{\bf hw}(\texttheta)}\})\iff G\setminus(Y\setminus D)\in{\sf excl}(\{K_{{\bf hw}(\texttheta)}\}),
which implies that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G[C]\in{\sf excl}(\{K_{{\bf hw}(\texttheta)}\})\iff G[C^{\prime}]\in{\sf excl}(\{K_{{\bf hw}(\texttheta)}\}).
Therefore,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}[C]\models\textmu\iff\mathfrak{A}[C^{\prime}]\models\textmu.

This concludes the proof of
Claim 3
.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\diamond

Recall that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\bullet}
be the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}g
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{1}

and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}^{\prime},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime})
be the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\bullet}
-tilt of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
given by the algorithm
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\tt Find\_Equiv\_FlatPairs}
in
Subsection 9.5
.
We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V:=V({\sf compass}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime}}(\tilde{W}^{\prime})).

Also, recall that the algorithm
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\tt Find\_Equiv\_FlatPairs}

outputs the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y=V({\sf compass}_{\breve{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime}}(\breve{W}^{\prime})),

where

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\breve{W}^{\prime},\breve{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime})
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{W}^{\prime}
-tilt of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{W}
is the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{1}.

Finally, recall that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}=g+2\hat{r}+2.

The definition of a tilt of a flatness pair implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V
is a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y.

By
Claim 1
, we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},X)\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\iff(\mathfrak{A}\setminus V,R\setminus Y,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.

Recall that since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y\subseteq I_{1}^{(w)},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\cap Y=\emptyset,
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)}\subseteq I_{2}^{(2)},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime\prime}\in{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{A}},{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})\iff C^{\prime\prime}\setminus V\in{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V,{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})
and, if

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal C}
(resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal C}^{\prime}
) is the set of all
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime\prime}\in{\sf cc}(G_{\mathfrak{A}},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})
(resp. all
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C^{\prime\prime}\in{\sf cc}(G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V,X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})
)
that are not in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{A}},{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})
(resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V,{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)},X_{\rm out}\cup X^{\prime})
), then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal C}={\cal C}^{\prime}.

Also, recall
that all the basic Gaifman variables in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}
are contained in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R
and every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textpsi_{i}({\sf x})
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{i}
-local.
The fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\bullet}
is the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}g
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{1},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{W}
has height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}g=j^{\prime}-2\hat{r}-2,
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\cap Y=\emptyset
implies that
these no local formulas
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textpsi_{i}({\sf x})
is evaluated using vertices in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V.

Therefore,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R\setminus Y,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C^{\prime}])\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}\iff{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A}\setminus V,R\setminus Y,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C^{\prime}\setminus V])\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R},
and, by
Claim 2
,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C])\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}\iff{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}((\mathfrak{A}\setminus V,R\setminus Y,{\bf a}^{\prime})[C^{\prime}\setminus V])\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}.

Finally, we observe that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}[C]\models\textmu\iff\mathfrak{A}[C^{\prime}\setminus V]\models\textmu.

Thus,
we get that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}\iff(\mathfrak{A}\setminus V,R\setminus Y,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

10
Dealing with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
be a vocabulary.
In this section we aim to present
how to generalize
the definition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{q},

given in
Subsection 9.1
,
and the
definitions of
out-signature and in-signature in
Subsection 9.3
and
Subsection 9.4

so as the algorithm
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\tt Fin\_Equiv\_FlatPairs}
(given in
Subsection 9.5
)
outputs the correct answer
for sentences in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}[\texttau].

Given a sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}[\texttau]
and an enhanced version
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}

of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta,

in
Subsection 10.2
,
we define the split version
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{q}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

Then, in
Subsection 10.3
, we generalize
the signatures given in
Subsection 9.3
and
Subsection 9.4
,
for a sentence in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}[\texttau]

and we describe how to prove
Subsection 8.1

for a sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}[\texttau].

The full proof of
Subsection 8.1

for a sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}[\texttau]
can be found in
Appendix B
.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h={\sf height}(\texttheta).

We fix
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf X}_{1},\ldots,{\sf X}_{h}
to be a set of
second-order variables.
Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textsigma\in{\sf FOL}[\texttau]
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textmu\in{\sf EM}[\texttau]

be the target sentences of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
and,
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[r],
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textbeta_{i}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}^{\bf tw}[\texttau\cup\{{\sf X}_{i}\}]

be a modulator sentence of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
and a string
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w=w_{0}\ldots w_{h-1}
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
whose alphabet is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\{\circ,\bullet\},

such that
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{0}=\textsigma\wedge\textmu,
and for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\texttheta_{i}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle=

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\textbeta_{i}\triangleright\textgamma_{i-1},

(22)

where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma_{i-1}=\texttheta_{i-1}^{({\sf c})},
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w_{i}=\circ
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma_{i-1}=\texttheta_{i-1},
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w_{i}=\bullet,

then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta=\texttheta_{h}.

In other words, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{i}
checks whether for a given
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A},

there exists a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}S_{i}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})
such that

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf star}_{{\sf X}_{i}}(\mathfrak{A},S_{i})\models\textbeta_{i}

and

•

depending whether
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma_{i-1}=\texttheta_{i-1}^{({\sf c})}
or
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{i-1}
:

–

either for every connected component
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus S_{i},
it holds that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}[C]\models\texttheta_{i-1},
or

–

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf rm}_{{\sf X}_{i}}(\mathfrak{A},S_{i})\models\texttheta_{i-1}.

We now generalize the notion of a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-flag given in the beginning of
Section 9
to the notion of a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-scenario.

10.1
Scenarios

The
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-scenarios.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}[\texttau]
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h={\sf height}(\texttheta).

We define the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-scenario
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
to be the string
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
whose alphabet is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\{\circ,\bullet\}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w_{i}=\circ
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma_{i}\in\bar{\textTheta}_{i}^{({\sf c})}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w_{i}=\bullet
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma_{i}\in\bar{\textTheta}_{i}.

Every sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}[\texttau]

is associated with its
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-flag.
Intuitively, the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-scenario of a sentence in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}
encodes whether each question, that is asked recursively in the structure obtained after removing a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{i},
concerns every substructure that corresponds to a connected component of the Gaifman graph of the remaining substructure or the union of all these substructures, in each “level” of the recursion.

Choosing the privileged set given by a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-scenario.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
be a graph, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q,h\in\mathbb{N},
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}\in{(2^{V(G)})}^{2q},
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}=\{X_{1},\ldots,X_{h}\}
be a collection of pairwise disjoint subsets of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(G).

Also, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
be a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-scenario of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h.

A sequence of subsets
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{1},\ldots,C_{h+1}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(G)
is called
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged sequence of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}
, if

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{h+1}=V(G)
and
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{i}\subseteq C_{i+1}
and

C_{i}=\begin{cases}\text{an element in }{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{q},X_{i}\cup\ldots\cup X_{h}),&\text{if }w_{i}=\circ\text{ and }{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{q},X_{i}\cup\ldots\cup X_{h})\neq\emptyset,\\
\emptyset,&\text{if }w_{i}=\circ\text{ and }{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{q},X_{i}\cup\ldots\cup X_{h})=\emptyset,\text{ and}\\
C_{i+1}\setminus X_{i},&\text{if }w_{i}=\bullet.\end{cases}

By
Subsection 7.3
,
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{q}
-pseudogrid of a graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X
is a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(G),
then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{{q}},X)
is either a singleton or the empty set.
Therefore, we get the following:

Observation 42
.

If
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{q}
-pseudogrid of a graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{1},\ldots,X_{h}
are subsets of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(G),
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-scenario of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h,
then there is exactly one
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged sequence of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\{X_{1},\ldots,X_{h}\}.

It is also easy to observe the following.

Observation 43
.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
be a graph, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q,h\in\mathbb{N},
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}\in{(2^{V(G)})}^{2q},
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}=\{X_{1},\ldots,X_{h}\}
be a collection of pairwise disjoint subsets of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(G).

Also, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
be a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-scenario of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h.

If
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{1},\ldots,C_{h+1}
is a

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged sequence of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\{X_{1},\ldots,X_{h}\},
then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq C_{h+1}
and if for some
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h]

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{i}=\emptyset,
then for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j\in[i]

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{j}=\emptyset.

A vertex set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\subseteq V(G)
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}
, if there is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged sequence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{1},\ldots,C_{h+1}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}
such that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{1}=C.

Due to
Subsection 10.1
, there is a unique
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}.

See
Figure 20
for an example.

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{5}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{4}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{1}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0.545,0,0.545}C_{2}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}C_{3}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.545}C_{5}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{1,0.549,0}C_{4}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{2}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{3}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0.392,0}C_{1}}

Figure 20
:
An example where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma_{5}\in\textTheta_{4},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma_{4}\in\textTheta_{3}^{({\sf c})},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma_{3}\in\textTheta_{2}^{({\sf c})},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma_{2}\in\textTheta_{1},
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma_{1}\in\textTheta_{0}^{({\sf c})}.
The sets
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{i},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[5]
are the vertex sets that are recursively removed from the graph, while the colored areas correspond to the vertex sets in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus X_{i}
that correspond to the “privileged part” of the graph.
Connected components of each level of the construction are depicted by dashed boundaries.
For example, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma_{5}\in\textTheta_{4},
then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{4}
is a vertex set in the graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus X_{5}
(which has three connected components - depicted by areas of dashed blue boundary), while, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textgamma_{4}\in\textTheta_{3}^{({\sf c})},
we search for a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{3}
in every component of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus(X_{4}\cup X_{5})
(the privileged connected component of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus(X_{4}\cup X_{5})
is the orange area that has dashed orange boundary).

It is easy to see that the notion of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set can be expressed in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mbox{\sf CMSOL}.

Lemma 44
.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q,h\in\mathbb{N},
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
be a vocabulary, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf Q}\cup{\cal X}\cup\{{\sf C}\},
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}=\{{\sf X}_{1},\ldots,{\sf X}_{h}\},
be a set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2q+h+1
new unary relation symbols,
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
be a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-scenario of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h.

There is a sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\cal X},{\sf C}}}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup{\cal X}\cup\{{\sf C}\}]
such that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A},
every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}\in{(2^{V(\mathfrak{A})})}^{2q},
every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal S}=\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{h}\}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
subsets of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\mathfrak{A}),
and every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},{\bf W}_{q},{\cal S},C)\models\texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\cal X},{\sf C}}}
(where every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf X}_{i}
is interpreted as
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}S_{i}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf C}
is interpreted as
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
) if and only if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}},
the sets in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal S}
are pairwise disjoint, and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal S}.

10.2
The sentences
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
be a vocabulary.
Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}[\texttau].

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h:={\sf height}(\texttheta).

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q,l\in\mathbb{N},
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf c}
be a collection of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l
constant symbols not contained in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau,
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\{{\sf R}\}\cup{\bf Q}\cup{\cal X},
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}=\{{\sf X}_{1},\ldots,{\sf X}_{h}\},
be a set of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2q+h+1
unary symbols not contained in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}
be an enhanced version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
be the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\circ/\bullet
-scenario of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta.

Τhe sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}.

We define the sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup\{{\sf R}\}\cup{\bf Q}\cup{\cal X}\cup{\bf c}]
such that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A},
every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}\in{(2^{V(\mathfrak{A})})}^{2q},
every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}),

every apex-tuple
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l,
and every collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal S}=\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{h}\}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
subsets of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\mathfrak{A}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},{\bf a},{\cal S})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}
if and only if the following conditions are satisfied:

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}},

•

the sets in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal S}
are pairwise disjoint, and

•

there exists a sequence

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{1},\ldots,C_{h}
of subsets of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\mathfrak{A})
that is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged sequence of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal S}
and

–

for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}S_{i}\subseteq C_{i+1},

–

for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}[C_{i+1}],S_{i})\models\textbeta_{i}|_{{\sf star}_{{\sf X}_{i}}},
and

–

for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h]
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w_{i}=\circ,
we have that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\in{\sf cc}(G_{\mathfrak{A}},S_{i}\cup\ldots\cup S_{h})
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\subseteq C_{i+1}\setminus C_{i},
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})[C]\models{\texttheta_{i-1}}_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

Note that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf R}\}\cup{\cal X}\cup{\bf c}],

since for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textbeta_{i}|_{{\sf star}_{{\sf X}_{i}}}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup\{{\sf X}_{i}\}]
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\texttheta_{i-1}}_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup\{{\sf R}\}\cup{\bf c}].

Intuitively, the sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}
does everything that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}
does, except from the privileged component.

The sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}.

We define the sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf R}\}\cup{\bf c}]
such that

\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}=\exists{\sf X}_{1},\ldots,{\sf X}_{h}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\framebox{$\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}$}}\ \wedge{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\framebox{$\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\exists{\sf C}\ \texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\cal X},{\sf C}}}\ \wedge\textmu|_{{\sf ind}_{\sf C}}$}}\ \wedge{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}\framebox{$\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\exists{\sf C}\ \texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\cal X},{\sf C}}}\ \wedge\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{{\sf ap}_{\bf c}}|_{{\sf ind}_{\sf C}}$}}

(23)

(recall that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup\{{\sf R}\}\cup{\bf Q}\cup{\cal X}\cup{\bf c}],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\cal X},{\sf C}}}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup{\cal X}\cup\{{\sf C}\}],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textmu\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau],
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{{\sf ap}_{\bf c}}\in{\sf FOL}[\texttau\cup\{{\sf R}\}\cup{\bf c}]
).

Alternatively, if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{{q}}\in{(2^{V(\mathfrak{A})})}^{2q},
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}
is an apex-tuple of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l,
then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},{\bf a})\models\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}\iff\exists S_{1},\ldots,S_{h}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})
such that, if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal S}=\{S_{1},\ldots,S_{h}\},
then

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\framebox{$\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},{\cal S})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}$}},

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\framebox{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}$\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\exists C\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})$ that is $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w$-privileged with respect to $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}$ and $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal S}$ such that $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}[C]\models\textmu$}}},
and

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\exists C\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal S}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})[C]\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{{\sf ap}_{\bf c}}.

We stress that in the above sentence, the part “
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\exists{\sf C}\ \texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\cal X},{\sf C}}}
”
appears twice, but essentially refers to the same set since, by
Subsection 10.1
, there is a unique
set in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\mathfrak{A})
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal S}.

Intuitively, if the Gaifman graph contains a “big enough” wall, we can “separate” the questions that concern all
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{i}
and every non-privileged connected component of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus(X_{i}\cup\ldots\cup X_{h})
(expressed by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}
) and the question
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textsigma\wedge\textmu
that concerns the privileged part of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus(X_{1}\cup\ldots\cup X_{h}).

We call
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{q}
the
split version
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

By using the same arguments as in the proof of
Subsection 9.1
, it is easy to prove the following:

Lemma 45
.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
be a vocabulary,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf R}\notin\texttau
be a unary relation symbol, and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf c}
be a collection of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l
constant symbols, where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}[\texttau],
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{q}={\sf height}(\texttheta)\cdot({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}+1,
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}
be an enhanced version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta,
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{q}
be the split version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

If
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{{q}}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}},

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}
is an apex-tuple of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l,

then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}\iff(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},{\bf a})\models\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}.

By
Subsection 10.2
, we also get the following:

Corollary 46
.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
be a vocabulary,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf R}\notin\texttau
be a unary relation symbol, and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf c}
be a collection of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l
constant symbols, where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}[\texttau],
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{q}={\sf height}(\texttheta)\cdot({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}+1,
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}
be an enhanced version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta,
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{q}
be the split version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

If
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)}
are two
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrids of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}},

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}
is an apex-tuple of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l,

then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},{\bf a})\models\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}\iff(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},{\bf a})\models\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}.

10.3
Modifying the signatures

In this subsection
we generalize the definitions given in
Subsection 9.3
and
Subsection 9.4
,
for a sentence in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}[\texttau].

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
be a vocabulary,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf R}\notin\texttau
be a unary relation symbol, and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf c}
be a collection of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l
constant symbols, where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}_{1}[\texttau],
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{q}={\sf height}(\texttheta)\cdot({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}+1,
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}
be an enhanced version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta,
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{q}
be the split version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

Recall that

\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}}=\exists{\sf X}_{1},\ldots,{\sf X}_{h}\ \bigg(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}\ \wedge\exists{\sf C}\Big(\texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\cal X},{\sf C}}}\ \wedge\textmu|_{{\sf ind}_{\sf C}}\Big)\wedge\exists{\sf C}\Big(\texteta_{w\text{-}{\sf pr}_{{\cal X},{\sf C}}}\ \wedge\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{\sf ap_{\bf a}}|_{{\sf ind}_{\sf C}}\Big)\bigg)

where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}=\{{\sf X}_{1},\ldots,{\sf X}_{h}\},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}[\texttau\cup\{{\sf R}\}\cup{\bf Q}\cup{\cal X}\cup{\bf c}],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{\sf ap_{\bf a}}\in{\sf FOL}[\texttau\cup\{{\sf R}\}\cup{\bf c}],
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textmu\in{\sf EM}[\texttau].

Recall that
there exist
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}p\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{p},r_{1},\ldots,r_{p}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1},
and sentences
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\textzeta}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\textzeta}_{p}\in{\sf FOL}[\texttau^{\langle{\bf c}\rangle}\cup\{{\sf R}\}]
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}
is a Boolean combination of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\textzeta}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\textzeta}_{p}
and for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h\in[p],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\textzeta}_{h}
is a basic local sentence with parameters
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell_{h}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{h},
i.e.,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\tilde{\textzeta}_{h}=\exists{\sf x}_{1}\ldots\exists{\sf x}_{\ell_{h}}\big(\bigwedge_{i\in[\ell_{h}]}{\sf x}_{i}\in{\sf R}\wedge\bigwedge_{1\leq i<j\leq\ell_{h}}d({\sf x}_{i},{\sf x}_{j})>2r_{h}\wedge\bigwedge_{i\in[\ell_{h}]}\textpsi_{h}({\sf x}_{i})\big),

where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textpsi_{h}
is an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{h}
-local formula in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf FOL}[\texttau^{\langle{\bf c}\rangle}]
with one free variable.
Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{r}:=\max_{h\in[p]}\{r_{h}\}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{\ell}:=\max_{h\in[p]}\{\ell_{h}\}.

For the rest of this subsection, keep in mind that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q=({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}+1.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}.

We set

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle j

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle=

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle{\sf odd}(\max\{q/2,j^{\prime}\}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle r

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle=

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle 2\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)\cdot\hat{r},\mbox{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle w

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle=

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle(r+2)\cdot q.

Towards constructing a boundaried structure.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q,j^{\prime},j,r,w,
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}l
as above.
Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
be a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau
-structure, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}
be its Gaifman graph, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{l})
be an apex-tuple of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}},
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
be a flatness pair of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V({\bf a})
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2w+j,
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{{q}}
be the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid defined by the horizontal and vertical paths of the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{q}
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W.

Also, let

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{K}=(\mathfrak{A}[V(K^{\bf a})],{\bf a},{\bf I},{\bf W}_{{q}})
be the extended compass
of the flatness pair
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V({\bf a})
and let

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell\in[0,h\cdot({\bf tw}(\texttheta)-1)].

Given a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}d\in[r,w],
a collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal Z}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h
subsets of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d-r+1)},
a collection of pairwise disjoint sets
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal L}=\{L_{1},\ldots,L_{h}\}
such that, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}L_{i}\in 2^{[l]},
and a collection of graphs
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal F}=\{F_{1},\ldots,F_{h}\}
such that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{i}\in{\cal F}^{V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})}_{\ell},

we define the graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K^{(d,{\cal Z},{\cal L},{\cal F})}
as the one obtained from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{K^{\bf a}[I^{(d)}\cup V_{\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal L}}({\bf a})]\cup\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal F}}
by making every vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal F}\setminus V_{\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal L}}({\bf a}))
adjacent to an arbitrarily chosen vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d)}\setminus\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal Z}
(see
Figure 21
for an example).

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d)}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0.545,0,0.545}F_{1}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L_{1}}({\bf a})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z_{1})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z_{1}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0.545,0,0.545}F_{2}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z_{2})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z_{2}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{F_{2}^{\prime}}
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L_{2}}({\bf a})
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{F_{1}^{\prime}}

Figure 21
:
The graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K^{(d,{\cal Z},{\cal L},{\cal F})}.

We also define the structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{(d,{\cal Z},{\cal L},{\cal F})}
to be the one obtained from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}[I^{(d)}\cup V_{\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal L}}({\bf a})]
after adding
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\sum_{F\in{\cal F}}|V(F)|
new elements to its universe and a binary relation symbol that is interpreted as pairs of elements (corresponding to the additional edges of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal F}
and every edge between a vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal F}\setminus V_{\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal L}}({\bf a}))
and a vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d)}\setminus\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal Z}
).
Notice that if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
is the Gaifman graph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A},
then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K^{(d,{\cal Z},{\cal L},{\cal F})}
is the Gaifman graph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{(d,{\cal Z},{\cal L},{\cal F})}.

The out-signature of an extended compass.

As in
Subsection 9.3
,
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell\in\mathbb{N},
let

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle{\cal H}^{(\ell)}=\{(H,{\cal V})\mid H\mbox{\penalty\ is a graph on $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell$ vertices and $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal V}$ is a nice 3-partition of $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}H$}\}.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}:=\texttau\cup{\bf Q}\cup\{{\sf R}\}\cup{\cal X}\cup{\bf c}
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf SIG}_{\sf out}}=\{({\bf H}_{1},\ldots,{\bf H}_{h},\bar{\textphi})

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\mid

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\exists\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{h}\in[0,h\cdot({\bf tw}(\texttheta)-1)]\mbox{\rm\penalty\ such that for every $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],$}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt{\bf H}_{i}\in{\cal H}^{(\ell_{i})}\mbox{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }\bar{\textphi}\in{{\sf rep}^{(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\ell_{i})}_{\texttau^{\prime}}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{{q}})}\}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{K}=(\mathfrak{A}[V(K^{\bf a})],{\bf a},{\bf I},{\bf W}_{{q}})
be the extended compass of a flatness pair
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a})
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2w+j,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R\subseteq V(K^{\bf a}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}d\in[r,w],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal L}=\{L_{1},\ldots,L_{h}\}\in(2^{[l]})^{h},
and a collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal Z}=\{Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{h}\}
of subsets of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I^{(d-r+1)}.

We define

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}}(\mathfrak{K},R,d,{\cal L},{\cal Z})=\{({\bf H}_{1},\ldots,{\bf H}_{h},\bar{\textphi})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf SIG}_{\sf out}}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\mid

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\mbox{for every $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],$\penalty\ }\exists\ F_{i}\in{\cal F}^{V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})}_{|V(H_{i})|-|\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z_{i})|},

such that if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf H}_{i}=(H_{i},{\cal U}_{i})
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal V}_{i}=(\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z_{i}),V_{L_{i}}({\bf a}),V(F_{i})\setminus V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})),

then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal V}_{i}
is a nice 3-partition

of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z_{i})\cup V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})]\cup F_{i}
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z_{i})\cup V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})]\cup F_{i}
is strongly

isomorphic to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}H_{i}
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\cal V}_{i},{\cal U}_{i}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\exists\ \mbox{an ordering $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}$ of $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bigcup_{i\in[h]}\big(\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z_{i})\cup V(F_{i})\big),$ and }

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\mbox{for every $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],$\penalty\ }\exists\ \tilde{X}_{i}\subseteq Z_{i}\cup V(F_{i}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mbox{\penalty\ such that\penalty\ }\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z_{i})\cup V(F_{i})\subseteq\tilde{X}_{i}\mbox{\penalty\ and}

if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}=\{\tilde{X}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{X}_{h}\}
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R^{\prime}=\bigcup_{i\in[h]}(Z_{i}\setminus\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z_{i}))\cap R),
then

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \big(\mathfrak{A}^{(d,{\cal Z},{\cal L},{\cal F})},R^{\prime},{\bf W}_{q},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X},{\bf b}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}\}.

Notice that if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h=1,
then the out-signature given here is exactly the out-signature defined in
Subsection 9.3
.

In-signature.

We define:

{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf SIG}_{\sf in}}=2^{[\ell_{1}]}\times\cdots\times 2^{[\ell_{q}]}\times[w].

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{K},R,d,{\cal L},{\cal Z}
as in the previous paragraph.
We set

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf in\mbox{-}sig}}(\mathfrak{K},R,d,{\cal L},{\cal Z})

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\{(Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{p},t)\in{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf SIG}_{\sf in}}\mid t\leq d\mbox{\penalty\ and\penalty\ $\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\exists\ C\in{\sf pr}(K^{\bf a}[I^{(d)}],{\bf W}_{q},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal Z})$ \penalty\ and}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \exists\ (\tilde{X}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{X}_{p})\mbox{\penalty\ such that\penalty\ }\forall h\in[p]\

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \tilde{X}_{h}=\{x_{i}^{h}\mid i\in Y_{h}\},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{h}\subseteq(I^{(t-\hat{r}+1)}\setminus\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal Z})\cap R,
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \text{if\penalty\ }{\bf a}^{\prime}={\bf a}\setminus V_{\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal L}}({\bf a}),\text{ then}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{h}\text{\penalty\ is\penalty\ }(|Y_{h}|,r_{h})
-scattered in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}(\mathfrak{A},{\bf a}^{\prime})[I^{(t)}\setminus\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal Z}]

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ and }{\sf ap}_{{\bf c}}(\mathfrak{A},{\bf a}^{\prime})[C]\models\bigwedge_{x\in\tilde{X}_{h}}\textpsi_{h}(x)\}.

We finally define

{\sf CHAR}=[r,w]\times(2^{[l]})^{h}\times 2^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf SIG}_{\sf out}}}\times 2^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf SIG}_{\sf in}}}

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K},R)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle=

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\{(d,{\cal L},{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf sig}_{\sf out}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf sig}_{\sf in}})\in{\sf CHAR}\mid\exists\ Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{h}\subseteq I^{(d-r+1)}\mbox{\penalty\ such that},

if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal Z}=\{Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{h}\},
then

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}}(\mathfrak{K},R,d,{\cal L},{\cal Z})={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf sig}_{\sf out}},\mbox{\penalty\ and\penalty\ }

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf in\mbox{-}sig}}(\mathfrak{K},R,d,{\cal L},{\cal Z})={\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf sig}_{\sf in}}\}.

Summary of the algorithm of
Subsection 8.1
for sentences in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{r}:=\max_{h\in[p]}\{r_{h}\}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{\ell}:=\max_{h\in[p]}\{\ell_{h}\}.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}c
to be the size of the
FOL
-target sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textsigma
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle q

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:={\sf height}(\texttheta)\cdot({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}+1,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\hyperref@@ii[@riguardavano]{f_{\ref*{@riguardavano}}}(|\texttheta|,{\bf tw}(\texttheta),g)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=\max\{q,(g+1)^{2}+1\},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\hyperref@@ii[@carthaginoise]{f_{\ref*{@carthaginoise}}}(|\texttheta|,{\bf tw}(\texttheta))

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=q-1,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle j^{\prime}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=g+2\hat{r}+2,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle j

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:={\sf odd}(\max\{q/2,j^{\prime}\}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle r

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=2\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)\cdot\hat{r},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle w

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=r\cdot q,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle m

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=2^{|{\sf CHAR}|}\cdot q\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3),\text{ and}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\hyperref@@ii[@occidentales]{f_{\ref*{@occidentales}}}({\bf hw}(\texttheta),{\bf tw}(\texttheta),c,l,g)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=\lceil(2w+j)\cdot\sqrt{m}\rceil.

We run the algorithm
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\tt Find\_Equiv\_FlatPairs}
presented in
Subsection 9.5
,
which finds a collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}=\{(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1}),\ldots,(\tilde{W}_{q\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{q\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)})\}

of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)
flatness pairs that are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent. i.e., their
extended compasses have the same characteristic.
Note that
the characteristic of each flatness pair, when dealing with a sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}[\texttau]
is defined using
the split version
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\texttheta}_{q}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}},
defined in
Subsection 10.2
,
and the signatures defined in
Subsection 10.3
.
The algorithm

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\tt Find\_Equiv\_FlatPairs}

also outputs
the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y:=V({\sf compass}_{\breve{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime}}(\breve{W}^{\prime})),
where

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\breve{W}^{\prime},\breve{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime})
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{W}
-tilt
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{W}
is the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{1},

and
a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\bullet}
-tilt

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}^{\prime},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime})

of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R}),

where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\bullet}
is be the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}g
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{1}.

The proof of why
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}\iff(\mathfrak{A}\setminus V({\sf compass}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime}}(\tilde{W}^{\prime})),R\setminus Y,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}
can be found in
Appendix B
.

Let us give a brief overview of the proof in
Appendix B
.
The existence of a
sequence
X of modulators (that, however, still have “small” bidimensionality to the given flatness pair)
give rise to a non-empty
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}.

Also, given that the bidimensionality of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}
with respect to the input flatness pair
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
is at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q-1,
there is
a “buffer” of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1})
that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}
does not intersect.
Again, we work with a flatness pair
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{2},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{2})\in\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1})
and
as in the case of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}_{1}[\texttau]
(
Subsection 9.6
),
the proof is split into three claims (
Claim 4
,
Claim 5
, and
Claim 6
).

The main difference that appears is that, when dealing with a sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta},
every
