\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal S}

if it is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textlambda
-homogeneous with respect to every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{A}\in{\cal S}.

The following observation is a consequence of the fact that, given a wall
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W
and a subwall
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\prime}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W,
every internal brick of a tilt
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\prime\prime}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\prime}
is also an internal brick of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W.

Observation 65
.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textlambda\in\mathbb{N},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
be a graph,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}A\subseteq V(G),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal S}\subseteq 2^{A},
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
be a flatness pair of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus A.
If
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textlambda
-homogeneous
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal S},
then for every subwall
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\prime}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W,
every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\prime}
-tilt of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
is also
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textlambda
-homogeneous
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal S}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a,\textlambda\in\mathbb{N}.

Also, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
be a graph,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}A
be a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(G)
of size at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a,
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,{\mathfrak{R}})
be a flatness pair of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus A.

For every flap
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F\in{\sf flaps}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W),
we define the function

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf var}^{(A,\textlambda)}_{F}:2^{A}\to\{\textlambda\mbox{\sf-folio}({\bf G})\mid{\bf G}\in\bigcup_{i\in[|A|+3]}{\cal B}^{(i)}\}

that maps each set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{A}\in 2^{A}
to the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textlambda\mbox{\sf-folio}({\bf F}^{\tilde{A}}).

The fact that there are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal O}(2^{a})
elements in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2^{A}
together with

Subsection A.6
implies the existence of an upper bound to the number of different
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textlambda
-folios of the augmented flaps of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,{\mathfrak{R}}),
as indicated in the following result.

Lemma 66
.

There exists a function
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}f_{16}:\mathbb{N}^{2}\to\mathbb{N}
such that if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a,\textlambda\in\mathbb{N},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
is a graph,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}A
is a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(G)
of size at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a,
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,{\mathfrak{R}})
is a flatness pair of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus A,
then

|\{{\sf var}^{(A,\textlambda)}_{F}\mid F\in{\sf flaps}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W)\}|\leq\hyperref@@ii[label_exiieriences]{f_{\ref*{label_exiieriences}}}(a,\textlambda).

Moreover,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hyperref@@ii[label_exiieriences]{f_{\ref*{label_exiieriences}}}(a,\textlambda)=2^{2^{{\cal O}((a+\textlambda)\cdot\log(a+\textlambda))}}.

Subsection A.6
allows us to define an injective function
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textsigma:\{{\sf var}^{(A,\textlambda)}_{F}\mid F\in{\sf flaps}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W)\}\to[\hyperref@@ii[label_exiieriences]{f_{\ref*{label_exiieriences}}}(a,\textlambda)]

that maps each function in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\{{\sf var}^{(A,\textlambda)}_{F}\mid F\in{\sf flaps}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W)\}
to an integer in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}[\hyperref@@ii[label_exiieriences]{f_{\ref*{label_exiieriences}}}(a,\textlambda)].

Using
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textsigma,
we define a function
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\zeta_{A,\textlambda}:{\sf flaps}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W)\to[\hyperref@@ii[label_exiieriences]{f_{\ref*{label_exiieriences}}}(a,\textlambda)],
that maps each flap
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F\in{\sf flaps}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W)
to the integer
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textsigma({\sf var}^{(A,\textlambda)}_{F}).

In
[
129
]
, given a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w\in\mathbb{N},
the notion of homogeneity is defined with respect to a
flap-coloring
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\zeta
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,{\mathfrak{R}})
with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
colors
, that is a function from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf flaps}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W)
to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}[w].

This function gives rise to the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\zeta\mbox{\sf-palette}
of each
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\mathfrak{R}}
-normal cycle of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf compass}_{{\mathfrak{R}}}(W)
which, in turn, is used to define the notion of a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\zeta
-homogeneous
flatness pair.
Hence, using the terminology of
[
129
]
,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\zeta_{A,\textlambda}
is a flap-coloring of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,{\mathfrak{R}})
with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hyperref@@ii[label_exiieriences]{f_{\ref*{label_exiieriences}}}(a,\textlambda)
colors, that “colors” each flap
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F\in{\sf flaps}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W)
by mapping it to the integer
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textsigma({\sf var}^{(A,\textlambda)}_{F}),
and the notion of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textlambda
-homogeneity
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2^{A}
defined here can be alternatively interpreted as
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\zeta_{A,\textlambda}
-homogeneity.
The following result, which is the application of
[
129
, Lemma 12]
for the flap-coloring
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\zeta_{A,\textlambda},
provides the conditions that guarantee the existence of a homogeneous flatness pair “inside” a given flatness pair of a graph.

Proposition 67
.

There is a function
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}f_{17}:\mathbb{N}^{3}\to\mathbb{N},
whose images are odd integers,
and an algorithm that receives as input an odd integer
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r\geq 3,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a,\textlambda\in\mathbb{N},
a graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G,
a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}A\subseteq V(G)
of size at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a,
and a flatness pair
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,{\mathfrak{R}})
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus A
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hyperref@@ii[@poderosamente]{f_{\ref*{@poderosamente}}}(r,a,\textlambda),

and outputs
a flatness pair
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\breve{W},\breve{{\mathfrak{R}}})
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus A
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r
that is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textlambda
-homogeneous with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2^{A}
and is
a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\prime}
-tilt of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,{\mathfrak{R}})
for some subwall
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\prime}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W.

Moreover,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hyperref@@ii[@poderosamente]{f_{\ref*{@poderosamente}}}(r,a,\textlambda)={\cal O}(r^{\hyperref@@ii[label_exiieriences]{f_{\ref*{label_exiieriences}}}(a,\textlambda)})
and the algorithm runs in time
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2^{{\cal O}(\hyperref@@ii[label_exiieriences]{f_{\ref*{label_exiieriences}}}(a,\textlambda)\cdot r\log r)}\cdot(n+m)
.

A.7
Canonical partitions

Canonical partitions.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r\geq 3
be an odd integer, let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W
be an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r
-wall, and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}P_{1},\ldots,P_{r}
(resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}L_{1},\ldots,L_{r}
) be its vertical (resp. horizontal) paths.
For every even (resp. odd)
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[2,r-1]
and every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j\in[2,r-1],
we define
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{A}^{(i,j)}
to be the subpath of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}P_{i}
that starts from a vertex of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}P_{i}\cap L_{j}
and finishes at a neighbor of a vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}L_{j+1}
(resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}L_{j-1}
), such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}P_{i}\cap L_{j}\subseteq A^{(i,j)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}A^{(i,j)}
does not intersect
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}L_{j+1}
(resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}L_{j-1}
).
Similarly, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i,j\in[2,r-1],
we define
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}B^{(i,j)}
to be the subpath of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}L_{j}
that starts from a vertex of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}P_{i}\cap L_{j}
and finishes at a neighbor of a vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}P_{i-1},
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}P_{i}\cap L_{j}\subseteq A^{(i,j)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}A^{(i,j)}
does not intersect
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}P_{i-1}.

For every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i,j\in[2,r-1],
we denote by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Q^{(i,j)}
the graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}A^{(i,j)}\cup B^{(i,j)}
and by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{Q_{\rm ext}}
the graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W\setminus\bigcup_{i,j\in[2,r-1]}Q_{i,j}.

Now consider the collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal Q}=\{Q_{\rm ext}\}\cup\{Q_{i,j}\mid i,j\in[2,r-1]\}

and observe that the graphs in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal Q}
are connected subgraphs of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W
and their vertex sets form a partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(W).

We call
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal Q}
the
canonical partition
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W.
Also, we call every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Q_{i,j},
for
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i,j\in[2,r-1],
an
internal bag
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal Q},
while we refer to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Q_{\rm ext}
as the
external bag
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal Q}.
See
Figure 26
for an illustration of the notions defined above.

Figure 26
:
A
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}5
-wall and its canonical partition
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal Q}.
The orange bag is the external bag
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Q_{\rm ext}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
be a flatness pair of a graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G.

Consider the canonical partition
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal Q}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W.
We enhance the graphs of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal Q}

so to include in them all the vertices of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
by applying the following procedure. We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}:={\cal Q}

and, as long as there is a vertex
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}x\in V({\sf compass}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W))\setminus V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}\tilde{\cal Q})
that is adjacent to a vertex of a graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Q\in\tilde{\cal Q},
update
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}:=\tilde{\cal Q}\setminus\{Q\}\cup\{\tilde{Q}\},
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{Q}={\sf compass}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W)[\{x\}\cup V(Q)].
Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf compass}_{{\mathfrak{R}}}(W)
is a connected graph, in this way we define a partition of the vertices of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf compass}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W)
into subsets inducing connected graphs.
We call the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{Q}\in\tilde{\cal Q}
that contains
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Q_{\rm ext}
as a subgraph the
external bag
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q},
and we denote it by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{Q}_{\rm ext},
while we call
internal bags
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
all graphs in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}\setminus\{\tilde{Q}_{\rm ext}\}.

Moreover, we enhance
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
by adding all vertices of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\sf compass}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W)
in its external bag, i.e., by updating
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{Q}_{\rm ext}:=G[V(\tilde{Q}_{\rm ext})\cup V(G\setminus V({\sf compass}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W))].

We call such a partition
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,{\mathfrak{R}})
-canonical partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G.

Notice that a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,{\mathfrak{R}})
-canonical partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
is not unique (since the sets in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal Q}
can be “expanded” arbitrarily when introducing vertex
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}x
).
We stress that every internal bag of a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,{\mathfrak{R}})
-canonical partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
contains vertices of at most three bricks of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W.

Appendix B
Details of dealing with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}

Here we present the details of the proof of
Subsection 8.1
for sentences in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}.

In
Subsection B.1

we present the algorithm
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\tt Find\_Equiv\_FlatPairs}

for sentences in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}
that is a genaralization of the algorithm presented in
Subsection 9.5
in the sense that
the characteristic of each flatness pair in the packing is defined as in
Subsection 10.3
.
Then, in
Subsection B.2
, we present the proof of correctness of the algorithm
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\tt Find\_Equiv\_FlatPairs}

for sentences in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}.

B.1
The algorithm for sentences in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}

The algorithm
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\tt Find\_Equiv\_FlatPairs}.

The algorithm has four steps.
First, recall that
there exist
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}p\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{p},r_{1},\ldots,r_{p}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1},
and sentences
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\textzeta}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\textzeta}_{p}\in{\sf FOL}[\texttau^{\langle{\bf c}\rangle}\cup\{{\sf R}\}]
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}
is a Boolean combination of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\textzeta}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\textzeta}_{p}
and for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h\in[p],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\textzeta}_{h}
is a basic local sentence with parameters
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\ell_{h}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{h},
i.e.,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\tilde{\textzeta}_{h}=\exists{\sf x}_{1}\ldots\exists{\sf x}_{\ell_{h}}\big(\bigwedge_{i\in[\ell_{h}]}{\sf x}_{i}\in{\sf R}\wedge\bigwedge_{1\leq i<j\leq\ell_{h}}d({\sf x}_{i},{\sf x}_{j})>2r_{h}\wedge\bigwedge_{i\in[\ell_{h}]}\textpsi_{h}({\sf x}_{i})\big),

where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textpsi_{h}
is an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r_{h}
-local formula in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf FOL}[\texttau^{\langle{\bf c}\rangle}]
with one free variable.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{r}:=\max_{h\in[p]}\{r_{h}\}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{\ell}:=\max_{h\in[p]}\{\ell_{h}\}.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}c
to be the size of the
FOL
-target sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textsigma
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle q

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:={\sf height}(\texttheta)\cdot({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}+1,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\hyperref@@ii[@riguardavano]{f_{\ref*{@riguardavano}}}(|\texttheta|,{\bf tw}(\texttheta),g)

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=\max\{q,(g+1)^{2}+1\},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\hyperref@@ii[@carthaginoise]{f_{\ref*{@carthaginoise}}}(|\texttheta|,{\bf tw}(\texttheta))

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=q-1,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle j^{\prime}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=g+2\hat{r}+2,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle j

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:={\sf odd}(\max\{q/2,j^{\prime}\}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle r

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=2\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)\cdot\hat{r},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle w

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=(r+2)\cdot q,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle m

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=2^{|{\sf CHAR}|}\cdot q\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3),\text{ and}

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\hyperref@@ii[@occidentales]{f_{\ref*{@occidentales}}}({\bf hw}(\texttheta),{\bf tw}(\texttheta),c,l,j^{\prime})

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle:=\lceil(2w+j)\cdot\sqrt{m}\rceil.

Step 1
:
We first find a “packing” of subwalls of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W,
i.e., a collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal W}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}m

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(2w+j)
-subwalls of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W
such that their influences are pairwise disjoint.
This collection exists because
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W
has height at least
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hyperref@@ii[@occidentales]{f_{\ref*{@occidentales}}}({\bf hw}(\texttheta),{\bf tw}(\texttheta),|\textsigma|,l,j^{\prime})=\lceil(2w+j)\cdot\sqrt{m}\rceil

and because, due to
Subsection A.4
, for every distinct
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{i},W_{j}\in{\cal W},
there are no cells of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{R}
that are both
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{i}
-perimetric and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{j}
-perimetric.
This can be done in time
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal O}(n)
.

Step 2
:
Then, for every wall
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{i}\in{\cal W},
we compute a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{i}
-tilt of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R}),
which we denote by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{i},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}),
and we consider the collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}:=\{(\tilde{W}_{i},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i})\mid W_{i}\in{\cal W}\}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}m
flatness pairs of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V({\bf a})
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2w+j.

Note that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}
can be computed in time
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal O}(n),
due to
Subsection A.4
.

Step 3
:
For every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[m],
let

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{i}:={\sf compass}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}}(\tilde{W}_{i}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{i}^{{\bf a}}:=G_{\mathfrak{A}}[V({\bf a})\cup V(K_{i})],
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(i)}
be the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid defined by the horizontal and the vertical paths of the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{i}.

Also, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}d\in[w],
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{i}^{(d)}:=V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}}(W_{i}^{(2d+j^{\prime})}))
and let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf I}_{i}:=(I_{i}^{(1)},\ldots,I_{i}^{(w)}).

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{K}_{i}:=(\mathfrak{A}[V(K_{i}^{\bf a})],{\bf a},{\bf I}_{i},{\bf W}^{(i)}_{{q}})
be the extended compass of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{i},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i})
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V({\bf a}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{i}:=R\cap V(K_{i}^{\bf a}),
and observe that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i,j\in[m],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{i}\cap R_{j}=R\cap V({\bf a}).

After defining the above collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\{(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1}),\ldots,(\mathfrak{K}_{m},R_{m})\}
of extended compasses of flatness pairs of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}\setminus V({\bf a}),
we compute their characteristics:
Since, by the hypothesis of the lemma,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{i},i\in[m]
has treewidth at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}z,
by Courcelle’s theorem (
Subsection 1.2
),
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{i},R_{i})
can be computed in time
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal O}_{|\texttheta|}(n).

We say that two flatness pairs
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{i},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}),(\tilde{W}_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{j})\in\tilde{\cal W}
are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{i},R_{i})=\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{j},R_{j}).

Step 4
:
Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}m=2^{|{\sf CHAR}|}\cdot q\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)
and for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[m],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{i},R_{i})\subseteq{\sf CHAR},

we can find a collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}\subseteq\tilde{\cal W}
of pairwise

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent flatness pairs
such that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}|\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}|=q\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3).

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1})\in\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{W}
to be the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{1},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\bullet}
to be the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}g
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{1},
and keep in mind that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}=g+2\hat{r}+2.

Note that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{W}
(resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\bullet}
) is also the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}
-subwall (resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}g
-subwall) of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{1}
and, therefore, it is a subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W
of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}j^{\prime}
(resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}g
).
Again, using
Subsection A.4
, we compute, in time
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal O}(n),

a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{W}
-tilt

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\breve{W}^{\prime},\breve{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime})

of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})

and
a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W^{\bullet}
-tilt

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}^{\prime},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime})

of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R}).

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y=V({\sf compass}(\breve{W}^{\prime},\breve{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime})).

We output the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Y
and the flatness pair

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}^{\prime},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime}).

bserve that the overall algorithm runs in linear time.

B.2
Proof of correctness of the algorithm for sentences in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bar{\textTheta}

In order to complete the proof of
Subsection 8.1
for a sentence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\in\bar{\textTheta}[\texttau],

we have to prove that

(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}\iff(\mathfrak{A}\setminus V({\sf compass}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime}}(\tilde{W}^{\prime})),R\setminus Y,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

For sake of simplicity, we use
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
to denote the Gaifman graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}}
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}.

Observations on the collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}.

Recall that, for every two
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{i},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}),(\tilde{W}_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{j})\in\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{i},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i})
(resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{j},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{j})
) is a

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{i}
-tilt (resp.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{j}
-tilt) of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R}),

where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{i},W_{j}\in{\cal W}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W_{i}))\cap V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W_{j}))=\emptyset.

This implies that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\sf compass}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{i}}(\tilde{W}_{i}))\cap V({\sf compass}_{\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{j}}(\tilde{W}_{j}))=\emptyset.

Moreover, observe that
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
is a

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
-canonical partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a}),

then no internal bag of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
intersects both
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W_{i}))
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W_{j})),
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i,j\in[m],i\neq j.

Shifting to the split version of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

Suppose that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})\models\texttheta_{{\sf R},{\bf c}}.

Recall that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}
is the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-pseudogrid defined by the horizontal and the vertical paths of the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{1}.

Due to
Subsection 10.2
, we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},{\bf a})\models\tilde{\texttheta}_{{q}},

i.e.,
there are sets
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{1},\ldots,X_{h}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})

such that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},{\bf a},X_{1},\ldots,X_{h})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}
and
there is a set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A})
that is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set of

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\{X_{1},\ldots,X_{h}\}

and it holds that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}[C]\models\textmu

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf a})[C]\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{\sf ap_{\bf c}}.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}=\{X_{1},\ldots,X_{h}\}.

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{1},\ldots,C_{h+1}
be the collection of vertex sets certifying
that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
is a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged set with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X},
i.e.,

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{1}=C,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{h+1}=V(G),
and

•

for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w_{i}=\circ,
then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{i}
is the
unique
element in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf pr}(G,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},X_{i}\cup\ldots\cup X_{h}),
while if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w_{i}=\bullet,
then
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{i}=C_{i+1}\setminus X_{i}.

By
Subsection 10.1
, the sequence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{1},\ldots,C_{h+1}
(and therefore also
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
) is defined uniquely.

We fix
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
to be a

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1})
-canonical partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a}).

The set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}
has bounded bidimensionality with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1}).

Notice that, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},{\bf a},X_{1},\ldots,X_{h})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}[C_{i+1}],X_{i})\models\textbeta_{i}|_{{\sf star}_{{\sf X}_{i}}}.

This, together with the fact that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h]

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textbeta_{i}\in\mbox{\sf CMSOL}^{{\bf tw}}[\texttau\cup\{{\sf X}_{i}\}]

imply that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf cl}_{{\sf X}_{i}}({\sf star}_{{\sf X}_{i}}(\mathfrak{A}[C_{i+1}],X_{i}))
has treewidth at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf tw}(\texttheta).

By the definition of the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged sequence
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{1},\ldots,C_{h+1}
and due to
Subsection 7.2
, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{i}
intersects at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}
internal bags of every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
-canonical partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a}).

Consequently,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}
intersects at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}h\cdot({\bf tw}(\texttheta)+1)^{2}=q-1
bags of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}.

Finding a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent extended compass that is disjoint from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{1},\ldots,X_{h}.

Recall that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}
is a collection of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)
flatness pairs of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a})

of height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2w+j
that are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1}).

The fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}
intersects at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q-1
bags of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}|\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}|=q\cdot(\hat{\ell}+3)

and,
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
is a

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(W,\mathfrak{R})
-canonical partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a}),

then no internal bag of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
intersects both
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W_{i}))
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\mathfrak{R}}(W_{j})),
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i,j\in[m],i\neq j,

implies that there is a collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime}
of size
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{\ell}+2
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1})\notin\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime\prime},
every flatness pair in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime\prime}
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta
-equivalent to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1}),
and the vertex set of its influence is disjoint from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}.

Assume, without loss of generality, that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{2},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{2})\in\tilde{\cal W}^{\prime\prime},
which implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1})=\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{2},R_{2})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(w)}\cap\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}=\emptyset.

Every modulator leaves an intact buffer.

We fix
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
to be a

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\tilde{W}_{1},\tilde{\mathfrak{R}}_{1})
-canonical partition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus V({\bf a}).

Note that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}
intersects at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q-1
bags of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}.

This implies that, given that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{W}_{1}
has height
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}2w+j,
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w=(r+2)\cdot q,

there is an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[q]
such that no set in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}
intersects
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{1}^{(i\cdot r-1)}\setminus I_{1}^{(i\cdot r-r)}.
We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}d=i\cdot r-1
and, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],
we set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{i}^{\rm in}=X_{i}\cap I_{1}^{(d-r+1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{i}^{\rm out}=X_{i}\setminus I_{1}^{(d)}.

Also, we set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}_{\rm in}=\{X_{1}^{\rm in},\ldots,X_{h}^{\rm in}\}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}_{\rm out}=\{X_{1}^{\rm out},\ldots,X_{h}^{\rm out}\}.

Picking the privileged component inside
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G\setminus(X_{1}\cup\ldots\cup X_{h}).

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}
be the privileged connected component of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}.

We stress that, the target sentences are asked to be satisfied in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
but, depending on whether
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w_{1}=\circ
or
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w_{1}=\bullet,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C
is either equal to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}
or
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C_{2}\setminus X.

For every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[2,h],
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z_{i}=(I_{1}^{(d-r+1)}\setminus C_{i})\cap C_{i+1}
and let

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z_{1}=(I_{1}^{(d-r+1)}\setminus\breve{C})\cap C_{2}.

Observe that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z_{i})\subseteq X_{i}^{\rm in}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{i}^{\rm in}\subseteq Z_{i}.

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal Z}=\{Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{h}\}.

All apices are adjacent to the privileged component.

For every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],
we also set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})=X_{i}\cap V({\bf a}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}L_{i}
to be the set of indices of the vertices of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf a}
in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{i},
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal L}=\{L_{1},\ldots,L_{h}\}.

Observe that, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})\subseteq X_{i}^{\rm out}.

We also claim that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\cup_{i\in[h]}V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})=V({\bf a})\cap N_{G}(\breve{C}).

To see why this holds, recall that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}
intersects at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q-1
bags of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
and that, by assumption,
every vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf a})
is adjacent, in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G,
to at least
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}q
internal bags of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}.

Therefore, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a\in V({\bf a}),
there is an internal
bag
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Q
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{\cal Q}
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(Q)\subseteq V(G\setminus X)
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a
is adjacent, in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G,
to a vertex in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(Q).

For every such
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Q,
since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}
is the privileged component with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X},

it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(Q)\subseteq\breve{C}
and therefore
every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a\in V({\bf a})
is adjacent, in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G,
to a vertex in

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}.

This implies that every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}a\in V({\bf a})
is either in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G}(\breve{C})
(that is a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}
) or belongs to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}.

Therefore
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L}({\bf a})=V({\bf a})\cap N_{G}(\breve{C}).

The fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1})=\texttheta\text{-}{\sf char}(\mathfrak{K}_{2},R_{2})
implies that there is a collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal Z}^{\prime}=\{Z_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,Z_{h}^{\prime}\}
of subsets of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(d-r+1)},
such that

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1},d,{\cal L},{\cal Z})={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}}(\mathfrak{K}_{2},R_{2},d,{\cal L},{\cal Z}^{\prime})
and

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf in\mbox{-}sig}}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1},d,{\cal L},{\cal Z})={\color[rgb]{0,0.7,0}{\sf in\mbox{-}sig}}(\mathfrak{K}_{2},R_{2},d,{\cal L},{\cal Z}^{\prime}).

We first prove the following:

Claim 4.

There is a collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}^{\prime}=\{X_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,X_{h}^{\prime}\},
such that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq Z_{i}^{\prime},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z_{i}^{\prime})\subseteq X_{i}^{\prime},
and for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V\subseteq Y

that is also a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\sf influence}_{\breve{\mathfrak{R}}^{\prime}}(\overline{W}),
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\overline{W}
is the central
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(j^{\prime}-2)
-subwall of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}W_{1},
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\iff(\mathfrak{A}\setminus V,R\setminus Y,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}_{\rm out}\cup{\cal X}^{\prime})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.

Proof of
Claim 4
:

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t=|N_{G}(\breve{C})|.

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}\in{\sf cc}(G,\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}),
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G}(\breve{C})\subseteq\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}.

For every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],
let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t_{i}=|N_{G}(\breve{C})\cap X_{i}|
and observe that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\sum_{i\in[h]}t_{i}=t.

Also, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],
since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf cl}_{{\sf X}_{i}}({\sf star}_{{\sf X}_{i}}(\mathfrak{A}[C_{i+1}],X_{i}))
has treewidth at most
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf tw}(\texttheta),

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G}(\breve{C})\cap X_{i}
induces a complete graph on
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t_{i}
vertices in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf cl}_{{\sf X}_{i}}({\sf star}_{{\sf X}_{i}}(\mathfrak{A}[C_{i+1}],X_{i})),

we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t_{i}\in[0,{\bf tw}(\texttheta)-1].

Therefore, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\sum_{i\in[h]}t_{i}=t,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t\in[0,h\cdot({\bf tw}(\texttheta)-1)].

Defining the boundary of our boundaried structure.

For every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],
we set

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{i}^{\prime}
to be the graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G[(X_{i}^{\rm out}\setminus V_{L_{i}}({\bf a}))\cap N_{G}(\breve{C})]
and

•

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{i}^{\star}
to be the graph obtained from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G[V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})\cup V(F_{i}^{\prime})]
after removing every edge that has both endpoints in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L_{i}}({\bf a}).

In other words,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{i}^{\prime}
is the subgraph of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G
induced by the vertices of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{i}^{\rm out}
that are not apices and are adjacent to vertices in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}.

Also, we extend
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{i}^{\prime}
to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{i}^{\star}
by adding the vertices in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})
and the edges connecting vertices of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F_{i}^{\prime}),
but not the edges that have both endpoints in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V_{L_{i}}({\bf a}).

This graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{i}^{\star}
will be later associated with a graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{i}\in{\cal F}_{t_{i}-|\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z_{i})|}^{V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})}.

Separating
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}
into two boundaried structures.

We use
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z^{\prime}
to denote
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal Z}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal Z}^{\prime},
respectively.
Also, we use
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\star}
to denote the graph
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}F_{i}^{\star}.

Let

\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star}=\mathfrak{A}\setminus(\breve{C}\cup(Z\setminus\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)))\mbox{\penalty\ \penalty\ and\penalty\ \penalty\ }\mathfrak{A}^{\star}=\mathfrak{A}[\breve{C}\cup Z\cup V(F^{\star})].

Keep in mind that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star})\cap V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star})=\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star}).

Next, we will define two boundaried structures corresponding to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{\star},
whose boundary will be the set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star}).

An ordering on the (common) boundary of the two structures.

We next claim that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star})=N_{G}(\breve{C}),
which directly implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}|\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star})|=t.

To see why
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star})=N_{G}(\breve{C}),
first recall that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G}(\breve{C})\subseteq\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}
and also notice that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}_{\rm in}\cap N_{G}(\breve{C})=\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z).

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\cup_{i\in[h]}V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})=V({\bf a})\cap N_{G}(\breve{C})
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\cup_{i\in[h]}V(F_{i}^{\prime})=(X^{i}_{\rm out}\setminus V_{L_{i}}({\bf a}))\cap N_{G}(\breve{C}),
we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}N_{G}(\breve{C})=\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup\bigcup_{i\in[h]}(V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})\cup V(F_{i}^{\prime}))=\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star}).

Therefore, we can consider an ordering
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}v_{1},\ldots,v_{t}
of the vertices in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z)\cup V(F^{\star}).

Let
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}^{\star}_{1}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{t})
and recall that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},R^{\circ}\cup R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf a}^{0})[C]\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{\sf ap_{\bf c}}\wedge\textmu

if and only if for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\in{\sf cc}(\mathfrak{B},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1})\setminus{\sf pr}(G_{\mathfrak{B}},{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},X^{\circ}\cup\tilde{X}_{1}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{B},R^{\circ}\cup R_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},{\bf a}^{0})[C]\models\breve{\textzeta}_{\sf R}|_{\sf ap_{\bf c}}\wedge\textmu.

Observe that, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\cap V(\mathfrak{A}_{1}^{\star}),
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{B}^{\star})\cap V(\mathfrak{B}).

Thus, item (1) above holds.

Recall that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}_{1}=\{\tilde{X}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{X}_{h}\}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}_{1}^{\star}=\{\tilde{X}^{\star}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{X}^{\star}_{h}\},
where
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{i}=(\tilde{X}^{\star}_{i}\setminus V(F_{i}^{\star}))\cup V(F_{i})
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h].

This implies that if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}^{\circ}\cup{\cal X}_{1}^{\star}=\{\hat{X}_{1}^{\star},\ldots,\hat{X}_{h}^{\star}\}
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}^{\circ}\cup{\cal X}_{1}=\{\hat{X}_{1},\ldots,\hat{X}_{h}\},

then the sets
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{X}^{\star}_{1},\ldots,\hat{X}^{\star}_{h}
are pairwise disjoint if and only if the sets
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{X}_{1},\ldots,\hat{X}_{h}
are pairwise disjoint.
Thus, item (2) above holds.

Βy
Subsection 10.1
, there is a unique
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged sequence of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{B}}
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\{\hat{X}_{1},\ldots,\hat{X}_{h}\}

and a unique

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged sequence of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{B}^{\star}}
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\{\hat{X}_{1}^{\star},\ldots,\hat{X}_{h}^{\star}\}.

Now notice that, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h]
and for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\in{\sf cc}(G_{\mathfrak{B}^{\star}},\hat{X}^{\star}_{i}\cup\ldots\cup\hat{X}^{\star}_{h})
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\subseteq\hat{C}^{\star}_{i+1}\setminus\hat{C}^{\star}_{i},

it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\subseteq Z.

Similarly,
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h]
and for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\in{\sf cc}(G_{\mathfrak{B}},\hat{X}_{i}\cup\ldots\cup\hat{X}_{h})
such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\subseteq{\hat{C}_{i+1}\setminus\hat{C}_{i}},

it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}C\subseteq Z^{\prime}.

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}^{\star}}[Z]
is the same graph as
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}G_{\mathfrak{A}_{1}}[Z],

we have that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\text{the set of all }C\in{\sf cc}(G_{\mathfrak{B}^{\star}},\hat{X}^{\star}_{1}\cup\ldots\cup\hat{X}^{\star}_{h})\text{ that are not subsets of }\hat{C}^{\star}_{1}=

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\text{the set of all }C\in{\sf cc}(G_{\mathfrak{B}},\hat{X}_{1}\cup\ldots\cup\hat{X}_{h})\text{ that are not subsets of }\hat{C}_{1}.

Also, notice that, by the definition of a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}w
-privileged sequence, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{X}^{\star}_{i}\subseteq\hat{C}^{\star}_{i+1}

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\hat{X}_{i}\subseteq\hat{C}_{i+1}.

Therefore, it remains to prove that
for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{B}[\hat{C}_{i+1}],\hat{X}_{i})\models\textbeta_{i}|_{{\sf star}_{{\sf X}_{i}}}\iff(\mathfrak{B}^{\star}[\hat{C}^{\star}_{i+1}],\hat{X}^{\star}_{i})\models\textbeta_{i}|_{{\sf star}_{{\sf X}_{i}}}.

To prove this, we will argue that, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],
the structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf star}(\mathfrak{B}[\hat{C}_{i+1}],\hat{X}_{i})
is isomorphic to the structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf star}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star}[\hat{C}^{\star}_{i+1}],\hat{X}^{\star}_{i}).

To see why this holds, notice that
there is a bijection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\textrho
from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\sf star}(\mathfrak{B}[\hat{C}_{i+1}],\hat{X}_{i}))
to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\sf star}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star}[\hat{C}^{\star}_{i+1}],\hat{X}^{\star}_{i})),
such that for all
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\sf R}
of arity
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}r\geq 1
and all
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf x}\in V({\sf star}(\mathfrak{B}[\hat{C}_{i+1}],\hat{X}_{i})),
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf x}\in{\sf R}^{{\sf star}(\mathfrak{B}[\hat{C}_{i+1}],\hat{X}_{i})}
if and only if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf x}\in{\sf R}^{{\sf star}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star}[\hat{C}^{\star}_{i+1}],\hat{X}^{\star}_{i})}.

Therefore,
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{B}^{\star},R^{\circ}\cup\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},{\bf a}^{\circ},{\cal X}^{\circ}\cup{\cal X}^{\star}_{1})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}\iff(\mathfrak{B},R^{\circ}\cup\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}},{\bf W}_{q}^{\circ}\cup{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},{\bf a}^{\circ},{\cal X}^{\circ}\cup{\cal X}_{1})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.
The subclaim follows.
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\diamond

By the above subclaim, we have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}\big)

are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},t)
-equivalent.
Therefore,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}\iff\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}.

(29)

Thus, we conclude that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{1},\ldots,F_{h},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}_{1},
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}_{1}

certify that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\bf H}_{1},\ldots,{\bf H}_{h},\bar{\textphi})\in{{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1},d,{\cal L},{\cal Z}).

Since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}}(\mathfrak{K}_{1},R_{1},d,{\cal L},{\cal Z})={{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}}(\mathfrak{K}_{2},R_{2},d,{\cal L},{\cal Z}^{\prime}),
we also have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\bf H}_{1},\ldots,{\bf H}_{h},\bar{\textphi})\in{{\sf out}\text{-}{\sf sig}}(\mathfrak{K}_{2},R_{2},d,{\cal L},{\cal Z}^{\prime}).

This implies that

(a).

for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],
there is an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{i}^{\prime}\in{\cal F}_{t_{i}-|\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z_{i}^{\prime})|}^{V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})}
such that if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal V}_{i}^{\prime}=(\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z_{i}^{\prime}),V_{L_{i}}({\bf a}),V(F_{i}^{\prime})\setminus V_{L_{i}}({\bf a}))

then

is a nice 3-partition of

and

is strongly isomorphic to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}H_{i}
with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\cal V}_{i}^{\prime},{\cal U}^{\prime})
and

(b).

there is an ordering
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}_{2}
of the vertices in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bigcup_{i\in[h]}\big(\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z_{i}^{\prime})\cup V(F_{i}^{\prime})\big)

and for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],
there is an
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq Z_{i}^{\prime}\cup V(F_{i}^{\prime})

such that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z_{i}^{\prime})\cup V(F_{i}^{\prime})\subseteq\tilde{X}_{i}^{\prime}
and,
if
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}_{2}=\{\tilde{X}_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\tilde{X}_{h}^{\prime}\}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R^{\prime\prime}=\bigcup_{i\in[h]}(Z_{i}^{\prime}\setminus\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z_{i}^{\prime}))\cap R_{2},
then

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{2}^{(d,{\cal Z}^{\prime},{\cal L},\{F_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,F_{h}^{\prime}\})},R^{\prime\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}.

Observe that, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{1}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z_{i})\cup V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})]\cup F_{i}

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{2}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z_{i}^{\prime})\cup V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})]\cup F_{i}^{\prime}

are strongly isomorphic to

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\cal V}_{i}^{\prime},{\cal U}_{i}),

we also have that

with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\cal V}_{i},{\cal V}_{i}^{\prime}).

We now set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}_{2}=\mathfrak{A}_{2}^{(d,{\cal Z}^{\prime},{\cal L},\{F_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,F_{h}^{\prime}\})}.

Notice that the fact that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{1}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z_{i})\cup V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})]\cup F_{i}

implies that the

-boundaried

are compatible.
Thus, given that

we have that

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}\big)
are

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},t)
-equivalent.
Therefore,

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}\iff\big(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R^{\prime\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}.

(30)

At this point, to give some intuition, we underline that even if

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R^{\prime\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}\big)

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{1},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}\big)
are

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},t)
-equivalent, we did not yet provide
a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structure that is a
substructure
of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X})
and that
is
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},t)
-equivalent to

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big).

To find such a substructure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R^{{}^{\prime\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star})
of the structure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}),

we have to “shift” from

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R^{\prime\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}\big)

to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R^{{}^{\prime\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}),

by replacing, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F_{i}^{\prime})
with
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F_{i}^{\star}),
and “extending”
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R^{\prime\prime}
to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R_{2}^{{}^{\prime}\star}

so as to contain all vertices in
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star}\setminus Y.

This substructure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R^{{}^{\prime\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star})
will replace
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)
in (
28
),
thus providing a collection
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}^{\prime}=\{X_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,X_{h}^{\prime}\},
such that for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}X_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq Z_{i}^{\prime},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z_{i}^{\prime})\subseteq X_{i}^{\prime},
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{{q}},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X})\models{\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}}\iff(\mathfrak{A},R\setminus Y,{\bf W}_{q},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}_{\rm out}\cup{\cal X}^{\prime})\models{\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}}.

Defining a substructure of the initial structure with a different boundary.

Let us now define the substructure
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R^{{}^{\prime\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star})
from

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R^{\prime\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}\big).

We set
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}
to be
the tuple obtained from
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}_{2}
after replacing, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],
each
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}v\in V(F_{i}^{\prime})
with the corresponding
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}u\in V(F_{i}^{\star}).

Also, let

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R^{{}^{\prime\prime}\star}=(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star})\setminus Y,
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}^{\star}_{2}=\{\tilde{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},\ldots,\tilde{X}_{h}^{{}^{\prime}\star}\},
where for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}\star}=(\tilde{X}_{i}^{\prime}\setminus V(F_{i}^{\prime}))\cup V(F_{i}^{\star}).

For the tuple
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R^{{}^{\prime\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star})
to be a
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structure,
we need to show that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R^{{}^{\prime\prime}\star}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}_{2}^{\star}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{2}^{\star})\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}).

Notice that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R^{{}^{\prime\prime}\star}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),
since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}R^{{}^{\prime\prime}\star}=(\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star})\setminus Y
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}).

To show that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{2}^{\star})\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),
we first notice that,
since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\breve{C}
respects
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)}
and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(d)}\cap\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}=\emptyset,

it holds that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(d)}\subseteq\breve{C}.

Therefore, since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z^{\prime}\subseteq I_{2}^{(d-r+1)},
we also have that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z^{\prime}\subseteq C.

The latter implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})
is a subset of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}).

By the definition of
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}
and since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{2})=\bigcup_{i\in[h]}\big(\partial_{\mathfrak{K}}(Z_{i}^{\prime})\cup V(F_{i}^{\prime})\big)=\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\cup\bigcup_{i\in[h]}V(F_{i}^{\prime}),
we have that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{2}^{\star})=\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\cup V(F^{\star}).

Hence, given that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z^{\prime})\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V(F^{\star})\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}V({\bf b}_{2}^{\star})\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}).

Also, observe that the fact that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}I_{2}^{(d)}\subseteq\breve{C},
implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\bf W}_{q}^{(2)}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),
while
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal X}^{\star}_{2}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}),
since
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}{\cal X}^{\star}_{2}=\{\tilde{X}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},\ldots,\tilde{X}_{h}^{{}^{\prime}\star}\},
where for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{i}^{{}^{\prime}\star}=(\tilde{X}_{i}^{\prime}\setminus V(F_{i}^{\prime}))\cup V(F_{i}^{\star}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\tilde{X}_{i}^{\prime}\setminus V(F_{i}^{\prime})\subseteq Z^{\prime},
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}Z^{\prime}\subseteq V(\mathfrak{A}^{\star}).

All considered boundaried structures are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},h)
-equivalent.

As a next step, we argue that the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structures

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R^{{}^{\prime\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}),

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R^{\prime\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}),

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)
are (pairwise) compatible.
To see why this holds, notice that, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],
since

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{1}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{1}}(Z_{i})\cup V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})]\cup F_{i}

is strongly isomorphic to

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}K_{2}^{\bf a}[\partial_{\mathfrak{K}_{2}}(Z_{i}^{\prime})\cup V_{L_{i}}({\bf a})]\cup F_{i}^{\prime}

with respect to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}({\cal V}_{i},{\cal V}_{i}^{\prime}),
it holds that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{i}
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{i}^{\prime}
are isomorphic. This, together with the fact that, for every
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}i\in[h],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{i}
is isomorphic to
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F_{i}^{\star}
implies that
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bigcup_{i\in[h]}F_{i}^{\prime},

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\bigcup_{i\in[h]}F_{i},
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}F^{\star}
are pairwise isomorphic graphs.
Therefore,
the structures
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{\star}[V({\bf b}_{2}^{\star})],

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}_{2}[V({\bf b}_{2})],
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\mathfrak{A}^{\star}[V({\bf b}_{1}^{\star})]
are (pairwise) isomorphic.

By following the exactly symmetric arguments as in the proof of the subclaim above, it is easy to show that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R^{{}^{\prime\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star})
and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R^{\prime\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2})
are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},t)
-equivalent. This implies that

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\displaystyle\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R^{{}^{\prime\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},\tilde{X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}\iff\big(\mathfrak{A}_{2},R^{\prime\prime},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}\big)\models\bar{\textphi}.

(31)

Therefore, combining (
29
), (
30
), and (
31
), we conclude that
the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t
-boundaried
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\texttau^{\prime}
-structures
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R^{{}^{\prime\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star})

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)

are
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q},t)
-equivalent.
Recall that, by (
28
),

(\mathfrak{A}_{\rm out}^{\star},R\setminus(\breve{C}\cup Z),\emptyset^{2q},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\rm out},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})\oplus(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star})=(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X})

and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A},R,{\bf W}_{q}^{(1)},{\cal X})\models\texttheta^{\sf out}_{q}.

Since the
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}t
-boundaried

-structures
\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},R^{{}^{\prime\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(2)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{2},{\bf b}_{2}^{\star})

and

\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.34}\big(\mathfrak{A}^{\star},\boldsymbol{\boldsymbol{\bigcup}}{\cal R}_{1}^{{}^{\prime}\star},{\bf W}_{{q}}^{(1)},\varnothing^{l},{\cal X}^{\star}_{1},{\bf b}_{1}^{\star}\big)

are
